v_{n}=\upgamma^{+}_{n}(0)\quad\text{and}\quad a_{n}=\varlimsup_{t\to\penalty 0+}{|{\upalpha(t)}-\penalty{\upgamma_{n}(t)}|}/{t}

then
a_{n}\to\penalty 0
and
v_{n}\to\penalty v
as
n\to\penalty\infty
.
Then

\upvarphi(v)=\lim_{n\to\penalty\infty}\upvarphi(v_{n}),

f\circ\penalty\upgamma_{n}(t)=f(p)+\upvarphi(v_{n}){\hskip 0.5pt\cdot\penalty\hskip 0.5pt}t+o(t),

|f\circ\penalty\upalpha(t)-f\circ\penalty\upgamma_{n}(t)|\leqslant\penalty{\ell}{\hskip 0.5pt\cdot\penalty\hskip 0.5pt}|{\upalpha(t)}-\penalty{\upgamma_{n}(t)}|.

Hence

f\circ\penalty\upalpha(t)=f(p)+\upvarphi(v){\hskip 0.5pt\cdot\penalty\hskip 0.5pt}t+o(t).

The last part follows from the definitions of differential and ultradifferential; see Section
4E
.
∎

H
Ultratangent space

Fix a selective ultrafilter
on the set of natural numbers.

For a metric space
\mathcal{X}
and
r>0
,
we will denote by
r{\hskip 0.5pt\cdot\penalty\hskip 0.5pt}\mathcal{X}
its
r
-blowup
,
which is a metric space with the same underlying set as
\mathcal{X}
and the metric multiplied by
r
.
The tautological bijection
\mathcal{X}\to\penalty r{\hskip 0.5pt\cdot\penalty\hskip 0.5pt}\mathcal{X}
will be denoted by
x\mapsto\penalty x^{r}
,
so

|{x^{r}}-\penalty{y^{r}}|=r{\hskip 0.5pt\cdot\penalty\hskip 0.5pt}|{x}-\penalty{y}|

for any
x,y\in\penalty\mathcal{X}
.

The
-blowup

\text{\rm}{\hskip 0.5pt\cdot\penalty\hskip 0.5pt}\mathcal{X}
of
\mathcal{X}
is defined to be the
-limit
of the
n
-blowups
n{\hskip 0.5pt\cdot\penalty\hskip 0.5pt}\mathcal{X}
; that is,

\text{\rm}{\hskip 0.5pt\cdot\penalty\hskip 0.5pt}\mathcal{X}:=\lim_{n\to\penalty\text{\rm}}n{\hskip 0.5pt\cdot\penalty\hskip 0.5pt}\mathcal{X}.

Given a point
x\in\penalty\mathcal{X}
, we can consider the sequence
x^{n}
, where
x^{n}\in\penalty n{\hskip 0.5pt\cdot\penalty\hskip 0.5pt}\mathcal{X}
is the image of
x
under
n
-blowup.
Note that if
x\neq y
, then

|{x^{\text{\rm}}}-\penalty{y^{\text{\rm}}}|_{\text{\rm}{\hskip 0.5pt\cdot\penalty\hskip 0.5pt}\mathcal{X}}=\infty;

that is,

x^{\text{\rm}}
and
y^{\text{\rm}}

belong to different metric components of
\text{\rm}{\hskip 0.5pt\cdot\penalty\hskip 0.5pt}\mathcal{X}
.

The metric component of
x^{\text{\rm}}
in
\text{\rm}{\hskip 0.5pt\cdot\penalty\hskip 0.5pt}\mathcal{X}
is called the
ultratangent space
of
\mathcal{X}
at
x
and is denoted by
\text{\rm T}^{\text{\rm}}_{x}\mathcal{X}
or
\text{\rm T}^{\text{\rm}}_{x}
.

Equivalently, the ultratangent space
\text{\rm T}^{\text{\rm}}_{x}\mathcal{X}
can be defined as follows.
Consider all the sequences of points
x_{n}\in\penalty\mathcal{X}
such that the sequence

(n{\hskip 0.5pt\cdot\penalty\hskip 0.5pt}|{x}-\penalty{x_{n}}|_{\mathcal{X}})
is bounded.
Define the pseudodistance between two such sequences as

|{(x_{n})}-\penalty{(y_{n})}|=\lim_{n\to\penalty\text{\rm}}n{\hskip 0.5pt\cdot\penalty\hskip 0.5pt}|{x_{n}}-\penalty{y_{n}}|_{\mathcal{X}}.

Then
\text{\rm T}^{\text{\rm}}_{x}\mathcal{X}
is the corresponding metric space.

Tangent spaces (see section
6E
) as well as ultratangent spaces
generalize the notion of tangent spaces on Riemannian manifolds.
In the simplest cases these two notions define the same space.
However in general they are different and are both useful —
often a lack of a property in one is compensated by the other.

It is clear from the definition that a tangent space has a cone structure.
On the other hand, in general an ultratangent space does not have a cone structure.
Hilbert’s cube
\prod_{n=1}^{\infty}[0,2^{-n}]
is an example.
We remark that Hilbert’s cube is a
\text{\rm CBB}({0})
as well as a
\text{\rm CAT}({0})
Alexandrov space.

The next theorem shows that the tangent space
\text{\rm T}_{p}
can be (and often will be) considered as a subset of
\text{\rm T}^{\text{\rm}}_{p}
.

6.17. Theorem.

Let
\mathcal{X}
be a metric space with defined angles.
Then for any
p\in\penalty\mathcal{L}
, there is a distance-preserving map

\upiota:\text{\rm T}_{p}\hookrightarrow\penalty\text{\rm T}^{\text{\rm}}_{p}

such that for any geodesic
\upgamma
starting at
p

we have

\upgamma^{+}(0)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\upiota}}{{\mapsto\penalty}}\lim_{n\to\penalty\text{\rm}}[\upgamma(\tfrac{1}{n})]^{n}.

Proof.

Given
v\in\penalty\text{\rm T}^{\prime}_{p}
,
choose a geodesic
\upgamma
that starts at
p
and such that
\upgamma^{+}(0)=\penalty v
.
Set
v^{n}=[\upgamma(\tfrac{1}{n})]^{n}\in\penalty n{\hskip 0.5pt\cdot\penalty\hskip 0.5pt}\mathcal{X}
and

v^{\text{\rm}}=\lim_{n\to\penalty\text{\rm}}v^{n}.

Note that the value
v^{\text{\rm}}\in\penalty\text{\rm T}^{\text{\rm}}_{p}
does not depend on the choice of
\upgamma
;
that is, if
\upgamma_{1}
is another geodesic starting at
p
such that
\upgamma_{1}^{+}(0)=v
,
then

\lim_{n\to\penalty\text{\rm}}v^{n}=\lim_{n\to\penalty\text{\rm}}v_{1}^{n},

where
v_{1}^{n}=[\upgamma_{1}(\tfrac{1}{n})]^{n}\in\penalty n{\hskip 0.5pt\cdot\penalty\hskip 0.5pt}\mathcal{X}
.
The latter follows since

|{\upgamma(t)}-\penalty{\upgamma_{1}(t)}|_{\mathcal{X}}=o(t),

and therefore
|{v^{n}}-\penalty{v_{1}^{n}}|_{n{\hskip 0.5pt\cdot\penalty\hskip 0.5pt}\mathcal{X}}\to\penalty 0
as
n\to\penalty\infty
.

Set
\upiota(v)=v^{\text{\rm}}
.
Since angles between geodesics in
\mathcal{X}
are defined, for any
v,w\in\penalty\text{\rm T}_{p}^{\prime}
we have

n{\hskip 0.5pt\cdot\penalty\hskip 0.5pt}|{v_{n}}-\penalty{w_{n}}|\to\penalty|{v}-\penalty{w}|
.
Thus
|{v_{\text{\rm}}}-\penalty{w_{\text{\rm}}}|=|{v}-\penalty{w}|
; that is,
\upiota\colon\text{\rm T}_{p}^{\prime}\to\penalty\text{\rm T}_{p}
is a distance-preserving map.

Since
\text{\rm T}_{p}^{\prime}
is dense in
\text{\rm T}_{p}
,
we can extend
\upiota
to a distance-preserving map
\text{\rm T}_{p}\to\penalty\text{\rm T}^{\text{\rm}}_{p}
.
∎

I
Ultradifferential

Given a function
f\colon\mathcal{L}\to\penalty\mathbb{R}
, consider the sequence of functions
f_{n}\colon n{\hskip 0.5pt\cdot\penalty\hskip 0.5pt}\mathcal{L}\to\penalty\mathbb{R}
defined by

f_{n}(x^{n})=n{\hskip 0.5pt\cdot\penalty\hskip 0.5pt}(f(x)-f(p)),

where
x\mapsto\penalty x^{n}
denotes the natural map
\mathcal{L}\to\penalty n{\hskip 0.5pt\cdot\penalty\hskip 0.5pt}\mathcal{L}
.
While
n{\hskip 0.5pt\cdot\penalty\hskip 0.5pt}(\mathcal{L},p)\to\penalty(\text{\rm T}^{\text{\rm}},0)
as
n\to\penalty\text{\rm}
,
the functions
f_{n}
converge to the
-differential
of
f
at
p
.
It will be denoted by
\bm{d}_{p}^{\text{\rm}}f
:

\bm{d}_{p}^{\text{\rm}}f\colon\text{\rm T}_{p}^{\text{\rm}}\to\penalty\mathbb{R},\quad\bm{d}_{p}^{\text{\rm}}f=\lim_{n\to\penalty\text{\rm}}f_{n}.

Clearly, the
-differential
\bm{d}_{p}^{\text{\rm}}f
of a locally Lipschitz subfunction
f
is defined and Lipschitz at each point
p\in\penalty\mathop{\rm Dom}\nolimits f
.

J
Remarks

Spaces with defined angles include
CAT
and
CBB
spaces;
see
8C
and
9C
.

For general metric spaces, angles may not exist,
and given a hinge
[p\,{}^{x}_{y}]
it is more natural to consider the
upper angle
defined by

\measuredangle^{\text{up}}[p\,{}^{x}_{y}]:=\varlimsup_{\bar{x},\bar{y}\to\penalty p}\text{\hbox to0.0pt{$\tilde{\text{\phantom{n}}}$\hss}{$\measuredangle$}}^{\upkappa}(p\,{}^{\bar{x}}_{\bar{y}}\hskip-0.86108pt),

where
\bar{x}\in\penalty\mathopen{]}px]
and
\bar{y}\in\penalty\mathopen{]}py]
.
The triangle inequality (
6C
) holds for upper angles as well.

Chapter 7
Dimension theory

A
Definitions

In this section, we give definitions of different types of dimension-like invariants of metric spaces and state general relations between them.
The proofs of most of the statements in this section can be found in the book of Witold Hurewicz and Henry Wallman
[
84
]
;
the rest follow directly from the definitions.

7.1. Hausdorff dimension.

Let
\mathcal{X}
be a metric space.
Its Hausdorff dimension is defined as

\mathop{\rm HausDim}\nolimits\mathcal{X}=\sup\left\{\,{\upalpha\in\penalty\mathbb{R}}\ {:}\ {\mathop{\rm HausMes}\nolimits_{\upalpha}(\mathcal{X})>0}\,\right\},

where
\mathop{\rm HausMes}\nolimits_{\upalpha}
denotes the
\upalpha
-dimensional Hausdorff measure.

Let
\mathcal{X}
be a metric space and
\{V_{\upbeta}\}_{\upbeta\in\penalty\mathcal{B}}

be an open cover of
\mathcal{X}
.
Let us recall two notions in general topology:

\diamond

The
order
of
\{V_{\upbeta}\}
is the supremum of all integers
n
such that there is a collection of
n+1
elements of
\{V_{\upbeta}\}
with nonempy intersection.

\diamond

An open cover
\{W_{\upalpha}\}_{\upalpha\in\penalty\mathcal{A}}
of
\mathcal{X}
is called a
refinement
of
\{V_{\upbeta}\}_{\upbeta\in\penalty\mathcal{B}}
if for any
\upalpha\in\penalty\mathcal{A}
there is
\upbeta\in\penalty\mathcal{B}
such that
W_{\upalpha}\subset\penalty V_{\upbeta}
.

7.2. Topological dimension.

Let
\mathcal{X}
be a metric space.
The topological dimension of
\mathcal{X}
is defined to be the minimum of nonnegative integers
n

such that for any open cover of
\mathcal{X}
there is a finite open refinement with order
n
.

If no such
n
exists, the topological dimension of
\mathcal{X}
is infinite.

The topological dimension of
\mathcal{X}
will be denoted by
\mathop{\rm TopDim}\nolimits\mathcal{X}
.

The invariants satisfying the following two statements
7A
and
7A
are commonly called “dimension”;
for that reason we call these statements axioms.

7.3. Normalization axiom.

For any
m\in\penalty\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant\penalty 0}
,

\mathop{\rm TopDim}\nolimits\mathbb{E}^{m}=\mathop{\rm HausDim}\nolimits\mathbb{E}^{m}=m.

7.4. Cover axiom.

If
\{A_{n}\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
is a countable closed cover of
\mathcal{X}
, then

\displaystyle\mathop{\rm TopDim}\nolimits\mathcal{X}

\displaystyle=\sup\nolimits_{n}\{\mathop{\rm TopDim}\nolimits A_{n}\},

\displaystyle\mathop{\rm HausDim}\nolimits\mathcal{X}

\displaystyle=\sup\nolimits_{n}\{\mathop{\rm HausDim}\nolimits A_{n}\}.

On product spaces.

Recall that the direct product
\mathcal{X}\times\penalty\mathcal{Y}
of metric spaces
\mathcal{X}
and
\mathcal{Y}
is defined in Section
2A
. Direct product satisfies the following two inequalities:

\displaystyle\mathop{\rm TopDim}\nolimits(\mathcal{X}\times\penalty\mathcal{Y})

\displaystyle\leqslant\penalty\mathop{\rm TopDim}\nolimits\mathcal{X}+\mathop{\rm TopDim}\nolimits\mathcal{Y}

and

\displaystyle\mathop{\rm HausDim}\nolimits(\mathcal{X}\times\penalty\mathcal{Y})

\displaystyle\geqslant\penalty\mathop{\rm HausDim}\nolimits\mathcal{X}+\mathop{\rm HausDim}\nolimits\mathcal{Y}.

These inequalities might be strict.
For topological dimension, strict inequality holds for a pair of Pontryagin surfaces
[
137
]
.
For Hausdorff dimension, an example was constructed by Abram Besicovitch and Pat Moran
[
26
]
.

The following theorem follows from
[
84
, theorems V 8 and VII 2]
.

7.5. Szpilrajn’s theorem.

Let
\mathcal{X}
be a separable metric space.
Assume
\mathop{\rm TopDim}\nolimits\mathcal{X}\geqslant\penalty m
. Then
\mathop{\rm HausMes}\nolimits_{m}\mathcal{X}>0
.

In particular,

\mathop{\rm TopDim}\nolimits\mathcal{X}\leqslant\penalty\mathop{\rm HausDim}\nolimits\mathcal{X}
.

In fact it is true that for any separable metric space
\mathcal{X}
we have

\mathop{\rm TopDim}\nolimits\mathcal{X}=\inf\{\mathop{\rm HausDim}\nolimits\mathcal{Y}\},

where the infimum is taken over all metric spaces
\mathcal{Y}
homeomorphic to
\mathcal{X}
.

7.6. Definition.

Let
\mathcal{X}
be a metric space
and
F\colon\mathcal{X}\to\penalty\mathbb{R}^{m}
be a continuous map.
A point
\bm{z}\in\penalty\mathop{\rm Im}\nolimits F
is called a
stable value
of
F

if there is
\upvarepsilon>0
such that
\bm{z}\in\penalty\mathop{\rm Im}\nolimits F^{\prime}

for any
\upvarepsilon
-close
to
F
continuous map
F^{\prime}\colon\mathcal{X}\to\penalty\mathbb{R}^{m}
,
that is,
|F^{\prime}(x)-F(x)|<\upvarepsilon
for all
x\in\penalty\mathcal{X}
.

The next theorem follows from
[
84
, theorems VI 1
\&
2]
.
(This theorem also holds for non-separable metric spaces
[
118
]
,
[
62
, 3.2.10]
).

7.7. Stable value theorem.

Let
\mathcal{X}
be a separable metric space.
Then
\mathop{\rm TopDim}\nolimits\mathcal{X}\geqslant\penalty m
if and only if there is a map
F\colon\mathcal{X}\to\penalty\mathbb{R}^{m}
with a stable value.

7.8. Proposition.

Suppose
\mathcal{X}
and
\mathcal{Y}
are metric spaces
and
\Phi\colon\mathcal{X}\to\penalty\mathcal{Y}
satisfies

|{\Phi(x)}-\penalty{\Phi(x^{\prime})}|\geqslant\penalty\upvarepsilon{\hskip 0.5pt\cdot\penalty\hskip 0.5pt}|{x}-\penalty{x^{\prime}}|

for fixed
\upvarepsilon>0
and any pair
x,x^{\prime}\in\penalty\mathcal{X}
.
Then

\mathop{\rm HausDim}\nolimits\mathcal{X}\leqslant\penalty\mathop{\rm HausDim}\nolimits\mathcal{Y}.

In particular, if there is a Lipschitz onto map
\mathcal{Y}\to\penalty\mathcal{X}
, then

\mathop{\rm HausDim}\nolimits\mathcal{X}\leqslant\penalty\mathop{\rm HausDim}\nolimits\mathcal{Y}.

B
Linear dimension

In addition to
\mathop{\rm HausDim}\nolimits
and
\mathop{\rm TopDim}\nolimits
,
we will use the so-called linear dimension.
It will be applied only to Alexandrov spaces and to their open subsets (in cases both of curvature bounded below and curvature bounded above).
As we shall see, in all these cases
\mathop{\rm LinDim}\nolimits
behaves nicely and is easy to work with.

Recall that a
cone map
is a map between cones respecting the cone multiplication.

7.9. Definition of linear dimension.

Let
\mathcal{X}
be a metric space with defined angles.
The
linear dimension
of
\mathcal{X}
(denoted by
\mathop{\rm LinDim}\nolimits\mathcal{X}
) is defined as the exact upper bound on
m\in\penalty\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant\penalty 0}

such that there is a distance-preserving cone embedding
\mathbb{E}^{m}\hookrightarrow\penalty\text{\rm T}_{p}\mathcal{X}

for some
p\in\penalty\mathcal{X}
; here
\mathbb{E}^{m}
denotes the
m
-dimensional Euclidean space
and
\text{\rm T}_{p}\mathcal{X}
denotes the tangent space of
\mathcal{X}
at
p
(defined in Section
6D
).

Note that
\mathop{\rm LinDim}\nolimits
takes values in
\mathbb{Z}_{\geqslant\penalty 0}\cup\penalty\{\infty\}
.

The linear dimension
\mathop{\rm LinDim}\nolimits
has no immediate relations to
\mathop{\rm HausDim}\nolimits
and
\mathop{\rm TopDim}\nolimits
.
Also,
\mathop{\rm LinDim}\nolimits
does not satisfy the cover axiom (
7A
).
Note that

\mathop{\rm LinDim}\nolimits(\mathcal{X}\times\penalty\mathcal{Y})=\mathop{\rm LinDim}\nolimits\mathcal{X}+\mathop{\rm LinDim}\nolimits\mathcal{Y}

➊

for any two metric spaces
\mathcal{X}
and
\mathcal{Y}
with defined angles.

The following exercise is based on a construction of Thomas Foertsch and Viktor Schroeder
[
144
]
;
it shows that the condition on existence of angles in
➊
cannot be removed.

7.10. Exercise.

Construct metrics
\uprho_{1}
and
\uprho_{2}
on
\mathbb{R}^{10}
defined by norms, such that
(\mathbb{R}^{10},\uprho_{i})
do
not
contain an isometric copy of
\mathbb{E}^{2}
but

(\mathbb{R}^{10},\uprho_{1})\times\penalty(\mathbb{R}^{10},\uprho_{2})
has an isometric copy of
\mathbb{E}^{10}
.

Remarks.

Linear dimension was first introduced by Conrad Plaut
[
136
]

under the name
local dimension
.

Geometric dimension
, introduced by Bruce Kleiner
[
94
]
is closely related;
it coincides with the linear dimension for
CBB
and
CAT
spaces.

One can extend the definition to arbitrary metric spaces.
To do this one should modify the definition of tangent space
and take an arbitrary
n
-dimensional Banach space instead of the Euclidean
n
-space.
For Alexandrov spaces (either
CBB
or
CAT
) this modification is equivalent to our definition.

Part II
Fundamentals

Chapter 8
Fundamentals of curvature bounded below

A
Four-point comparison

Recall (Section
6B
) that the model angle
\text{\hbox to0.0pt{$\tilde{\text{\phantom{n}}}$\hss}{$\measuredangle$}}^{\upkappa}(p\,{}^{x}_{y}\hskip-0.86108pt)
is defined if

|{p}-\penalty{x}|+|{p}-\penalty{y}|+|{x}-\penalty{y}|<\varpi{}^{{\upkappa}};

here
\varpi{}^{{\upkappa}}
denotes the diameter of model space
\mathbb{M}^{2}({\upkappa})
.

8.1. Four-point comparison.

A quadruple of points
p,x^{1},x^{2},x^{3}
in a metric space satisfies
\text{\rm CBB}({\upkappa})
comparison

if

\text{\hbox to0.0pt{$\tilde{\text{\phantom{n}}}$\hss}{$\measuredangle$}}^{\upkappa}(p\,{}^{x^{1}}_{x^{2}}\hskip-0.86108pt)+\text{\hbox to0.0pt{$\tilde{\text{\phantom{n}}}$\hss}{$\measuredangle$}}^{\upkappa}(p\,{}^{x^{2}}_{x^{3}}\hskip-0.86108pt)+\text{\hbox to0.0pt{$\tilde{\text{\phantom{n}}}$\hss}{$\measuredangle$}}^{\upkappa}(p\,{}^{x^{3}}_{x^{1}}\hskip-0.86108pt)\leqslant\penalty 2{\hskip 0.5pt\cdot\penalty\hskip 0.5pt}\uppi.

➊

or at least one of the model angles
\text{\hbox to0.0pt{$\tilde{\text{\phantom{n}}}$\hss}{$\measuredangle$}}^{\upkappa}(p\,{}^{x^{i}}_{x^{j}}\hskip-0.86108pt)
is not defined.

8.2. Definition.

Let
\mathcal{L}
be a metric space.

(a)

\mathcal{L}
is

\text{\rm CBB}({\upkappa})

if any quadruple
p,x^{1},x^{2},x^{3}\in\penalty\mathcal{L}
satisfies
\text{\rm CBB}({\upkappa})
comparison.

(b)

\mathcal{L}
is

locally
\text{\rm CBB}({\upkappa})

if any point
q\in\penalty\mathcal{L}
admits a neighborhood
\Omega\ni q
such that any quadruple
p,x^{1},x^{2},x^{3}\in\penalty\Omega
satisfies
\text{\rm CBB}({\upkappa})
comparison.

(c)

\mathcal{L}
is a

CBB
space if
\mathcal{L}
is
\text{\rm CBB}({\upkappa})
for some
\upkappa\in\penalty\mathbb{R}
.

Remarks

\diamond

\text{\rm CBB}({\upkappa})
length spaces are often called
spaces with curvature
\geqslant\penalty\upkappa
in the sense of Alexandrov
. These spaces will usually be denoted by
\mathcal{L}
, for
\mathcal{L}
ower curvature bound.

\diamond

In the definition of
\text{\rm CBB}({\upkappa})
, when
\upkappa>0
most authors assume in addition that the diameter is at most the model diameter
\varpi{}^{{\upkappa}}
.
For a complete length space, the latter means that it is not isometric to one of the exceptional spaces, see
8H
.
We do not make this assumption.
In particular, we consider the real line to have curvature
\geqslant\penalty 1
.

\diamond

If
\upkappa<\mathrm{K}
, then any complete length
\text{\rm CBB}({\mathrm{K}})
space is
\text{\rm CBB}({\upkappa})
.
Moreover directly from the definition it follows that if
\mathrm{K}\leqslant\penalty 0
, then any
\text{\rm CBB}({\mathrm{K}})
space is
\text{\rm CBB}({\upkappa})
.
However, in the case
\mathrm{K}>0
the latter statement does not hold and the former statement is not trivial; it will be proved in
8F
.

8.3. Exercise.

Let
\mathcal{L}
be a metric space and
\upkappa\leqslant\penalty 0
.
Show that
\mathcal{L}
is
\text{\rm CBB}({\upkappa})

if for any quadruple of points
p,x^{1},x^{2},x^{3}\in\penalty\mathcal{L}

there is a quadruple of points
q,y^{1},y^{2},y^{3}\in\penalty\mathbb{M}^{2}({\upkappa})

such that

|{p}-\penalty{x^{i}}|=|{q}-\penalty{y^{i}}|\quad\text{and}\quad|{x^{i}}-\penalty{x^{j}}|\leqslant\penalty|{y^{i}}-\penalty{y^{j}}|

for all
i
and
j
.

The exercise above is a special case of (1+
n
)-point comparison (
10B
).

Recall that
denotes a selective ultrafilter on
\mathbb{N}
, which is fixed once and for all.
The following proposition follows directly from the definition of
\text{\rm CBB}({\upkappa})
comparison and the definitions of
-limit and
-power given in Section
4B
.

8.4. Proposition.

Let
\mathcal{L}_{n}
be a
\text{\rm CBB}({\upkappa_{n}})
space for each
n
.
Assume
\mathcal{L}_{n}\to\penalty\mathcal{L}_{\text{\rm}}

and
\upkappa_{n}\to\penalty\upkappa_{\text{\rm}}
as
n\to\penalty\text{\rm}
.
Then
\mathcal{L}_{\text{\rm}}
is
\text{\rm CBB}({\upkappa_{\text{\rm}}})
.

Moreover, a metric space
\mathcal{L}
is
\text{\rm CBB}({\upkappa})
if and only if so is
its ultrapower
\mathcal{L}^{\text{\rm}}
.

8.5. Theorem.

Let
\mathcal{L}
be a
\text{\rm CBB}({\upkappa})
space,
\mathcal{M}
be a metric space, and
\upsigma\colon\mathcal{L}\to\penalty\mathcal{M}
be a submetry.
Assume
p,x^{1},x^{2},x^{3}
is a quadruple of points in
\mathcal{M}
such that

|{p}-\penalty{x^{i}}|<\tfrac{\varpi{}^{{\upkappa}}}{2}
for any
i
.
Then the quadruple satisfies
\text{\rm CBB}({\upkappa})
comparison.

In particular,

(a)

The space
\mathcal{M}
is locally
\text{\rm CBB}({\upkappa})
.
Moreover, any open ball of radius
\tfrac{\varpi{}^{{\upkappa}}}{4}
in
\mathcal{M}
is
\text{\rm CBB}({\upkappa})
.

(b)

If
\upkappa\leqslant\penalty 0
, then
\mathcal{M}
is
\text{\rm CBB}({\upkappa})
.

Corollary
8F
gives a stronger statement; it states that if
\mathcal{L}
is a complete length space, then
\mathcal{M}
is always
\text{\rm CBB}({\upkappa})
.
The theorem above together with Proposition
3C

imply the following:

8.6. Corollary.

Assume that the group
G
acts isometrically on a
\text{\rm CBB}({\upkappa})
space
\mathcal{L}
and has closed orbits.
Then the quotient space
\mathcal{L}/G
is locally
\text{\rm CBB}({\upkappa})
.

8.7. Example.

If
G<\mathrm{O}(n+1)
is a closed subgroup, then
\mathbb{S}^{n}/G
is
\text{\rm CBB}({1})
and
\mathbb{R}^{n+1}/G
is
\text{\rm CBB}({0})
.

Proof of
8A
.

Fix a quadruple of points
p,x^{1},x^{2},x^{3}\in\penalty\mathcal{M}
such that

|{p}-\penalty{x^{i}}|<\tfrac{\varpi{}^{{\upkappa}}}{2}
for any
i
.
Choose an arbitrary
\hat{p}\in\penalty\mathcal{L}
such that
\upsigma(\hat{p})=p
.

Since
\upsigma
is submetry, we can choose the points
\hat{x}^{1},\hat{x}^{2},\hat{x}^{3}\in\penalty\mathcal{L}
such that
\upsigma(\hat{x}_{i})=x_{i}
and

|{p}-\penalty{x^{i}}|_{\mathcal{M}}\lessgtr|{\hat{p}}-\penalty{\hat{x}^{i}}|_{\mathcal{L}}\pm\updelta

for all
i
and any fixed
\updelta>0
.

Note that

|{x^{i}}-\penalty{x^{j}}|_{\mathcal{M}}\leqslant\penalty|{\hat{x}^{i}}-\penalty{\hat{x}^{j}}|_{\mathcal{L}}\leqslant\penalty|{p}-\penalty{x^{i}}|_{\mathcal{M}}+|{p}-\penalty{x^{j}}|_{\mathcal{M}}+2{\hskip 0.5pt\cdot\penalty\hskip 0.5pt}\updelta

for all
i
and
j
.

Since
|{p}-\penalty{x^{i}}|<\tfrac{\varpi{}^{{\upkappa}}}{2}
,
we can choose
\updelta>0
above so that the angles
\text{\hbox to0.0pt{$\tilde{\text{\phantom{n}}}$\hss}{$\measuredangle$}}^{\upkappa}(\hat{p}\,{}^{\hat{x}^{i}}_{\hat{x}^{j}}\hskip-0.86108pt)
are defined.
Moreover, given
\upvarepsilon>0
, the value
\updelta
can be chosen in such a way that the inequality

\text{\hbox to0.0pt{$\tilde{\text{\phantom{n}}}$\hss}{$\measuredangle$}}^{\upkappa}(p\,{}^{x^{i}}_{x^{j}}\hskip-0.86108pt)<\text{\hbox to0.0pt{$\tilde{\text{\phantom{n}}}$\hss}{$\measuredangle$}}^{\upkappa}(\hat{p}\,{}^{\hat{x}^{i}}_{\hat{x}^{j}}\hskip-0.86108pt)+\upvarepsilon

➋

holds for all
i
and
j
.

By
\text{\rm CBB}({\upkappa})
comparison in
\mathcal{L}
,
we have

\text{\hbox to0.0pt{$\tilde{\text{\phantom{n}}}$\hss}{$\measuredangle$}}^{\upkappa}(\hat{p}\,{}^{\hat{x}^{1}}_{\hat{x}^{2}}\hskip-0.86108pt)+\text{\hbox to0.0pt{$\tilde{\text{\phantom{n}}}$\hss}{$\measuredangle$}}^{\upkappa}(\hat{p}\,{}^{\hat{x}^{2}}_{\hat{x}^{3}}\hskip-0.86108pt)+\text{\hbox to0.0pt{$\tilde{\text{\phantom{n}}}$\hss}{$\measuredangle$}}^{\upkappa}(\hat{p}\,{}^{\hat{x}^{3}}_{\hat{x}^{1}}\hskip-0.86108pt)\leqslant\penalty 2{\hskip 0.5pt\cdot\penalty\hskip 0.5pt}\uppi.

Applying
➋
,
we get

\text{\hbox to0.0pt{$\tilde{\text{\phantom{n}}}$\hss}{$\measuredangle$}}^{\upkappa}(p\,{}^{x^{1}}_{x^{2}}\hskip-0.86108pt)+\text{\hbox to0.0pt{$\tilde{\text{\phantom{n}}}$\hss}{$\measuredangle$}}^{\upkappa}(p\,{}^{x^{2}}_{x^{3}}\hskip-0.86108pt)+\text{\hbox to0.0pt{$\tilde{\text{\phantom{n}}}$\hss}{$\measuredangle$}}^{\upkappa}(p\,{}^{x^{3}}_{x^{1}}\hskip-0.86108pt)<2{\hskip 0.5pt\cdot\penalty\hskip 0.5pt}\uppi+3{\hskip 0.5pt\cdot\penalty\hskip 0.5pt}\upvarepsilon.

Since
\upvarepsilon>0
is arbitrary we have

\text{\hbox to0.0pt{$\tilde{\text{\phantom{n}}}$\hss}{$\measuredangle$}}^{\upkappa}(p\,{}^{x^{1}}_{x^{2}}\hskip-0.86108pt)+\text{\hbox to0.0pt{$\tilde{\text{\phantom{n}}}$\hss}{$\measuredangle$}}^{\upkappa}(p\,{}^{x^{2}}_{x^{3}}\hskip-0.86108pt)+\text{\hbox to0.0pt{$\tilde{\text{\phantom{n}}}$\hss}{$\measuredangle$}}^{\upkappa}(p\,{}^{x^{3}}_{x^{1}}\hskip-0.86108pt)\leqslant\penalty 2{\hskip 0.5pt\cdot\penalty\hskip 0.5pt}\uppi;

that is,
the
\text{\rm CBB}({\upkappa})
comparison holds for this quadruple in
\mathcal{M}
.
∎

B
Geodesics

Recall that general complete length spaces might have no geodesics;
see Exercise
2C
.

8.8. Exercise.

Construct a complete length
\text{\rm CBB}({0})
space that is not geodesic.

We are going to show that all complete length
CBB
spaces have plenty of geodesics in the following sense. Recall that a subset of a topological space is called G-delta if it is a countable intersection of open sets.

8.9. Definition.

A metric space
\mathcal{X}
is called
G-delta geodesic

if for any point
p\in\penalty\mathcal{X}
there is a dense G-delta set
W_{p}\subset\penalty\mathcal{X}
such that for any
q\in\penalty W_{p}
there is a geodesic
[pq]
.

A metric space
\mathcal{X}
is called
locally G-delta geodesic

if for any point
p\in\penalty\mathcal{X}
there is a G-delta set
W_{p}\subset\penalty\mathcal{X}
such that

W_{p}
is dense in a neighborhood of
p

and for any
q\in\penalty W_{p}
there is a geodesic
[pq]
.

8.10. Definition.

Let
\mathcal{X}
be a metric space
and
p\in\penalty\mathcal{X}
.
A point
q\in\penalty\mathcal{X}
is called
p
-straight
(briefly,
q\in\penalty\mathop{\rm Str}\nolimits(p)
) if

\varlimsup_{r\to\penalty q}\frac{|{p}-\penalty{r}|-|{p}-\penalty{q}|}{|{q}-\penalty{r}|}=1.

For an array of points
x^{1},x^{2},\dots\penalty,x^{k}
,
we use the notation

\mathop{\rm Str}\nolimits(x^{1},x^{2},\dots\penalty,x^{k})=\bigcap_{i}\mathop{\rm Str}\nolimits(x^{i}).

8.11. Theorem.

Let
\mathcal{L}
be a complete length
CBB
space and
p\in\penalty\mathcal{L}
.
Then the set
\mathop{\rm Str}\nolimits(p)
is a dense G-delta set.
Moreover, for any
q\in\penalty\mathop{\rm Str}\nolimits(p)
there is a unique geodesic
[pq]
.

In particular,
\mathcal{L}
is G-delta geodesic.

This theorem was proved by by Conrad Plaut
[
136
, Th. 27]
.

Proof.

Given a positive integer
n
,
consider the set
\Omega_{n}
of all points
q\in\penalty\mathcal{L}
such that

(1-\tfrac{1}{n}){\hskip 0.5pt\cdot\penalty\hskip 0.5pt}|{q}-\penalty{r}|<|{p}-\penalty{r}|-|{p}-\penalty{q}|<\tfrac{1}{n}

for some
r\in\penalty\mathcal{L}
.
Clearly
\Omega_{n}
is open;
let us show that
\Omega_{n}
is dense in
\mathcal{L}
.

Assuming the contrary, there is a point
x\in\penalty\mathcal{L}
such that

\mathrm{B}{}(x,\upvarepsilon)\cap\penalty\Omega_{n}=\varnothing

for
\upvarepsilon>0
.
Since
\mathcal{L}
is a length space,
for any
\updelta>0
, there exists a point
y\in\penalty\mathcal{L}
such that

|{x}-\penalty{y}|<\tfrac{\upvarepsilon}{2}+\updelta\quad\text{and}\quad|{p}-\penalty{y}|<|{p}-\penalty{x}|-\tfrac{\upvarepsilon}{2}+\updelta.

If
\upvarepsilon
and
\updelta
are sufficiently small, then

(1-\tfrac{1}{n}){\hskip 0.5pt\cdot\penalty\hskip 0.5pt}|{y}-\penalty{x}|<|{p}-\penalty{x}|-|{p}-\penalty{y}|<\tfrac{1}{n};

that is,
y\in\penalty\Omega_{n}
,
a contradiction.

Note that
\mathop{\rm Str}\nolimits(p)=\bigcap_{n}\Omega_{n}
;
therefore,
\mathop{\rm Str}\nolimits(p)
is a dense G-delta set.

Assuming
q\in\penalty\mathop{\rm Str}\nolimits(p)
,
let us show that there is a unique geodesic connecting
p
and
q
.
Note that it is sufficient to show that for all sufficiently small

t>0
there is a unique point
z
such that

t=|{q}-\penalty{z}|=|{p}-\penalty{q}|-|{p}-\penalty{z}|.

➊

First let us show uniqueness.
Assume
z
and
z^{\prime}
both satisfy
➊
.
Take a sequence
r_{n}\to\penalty q
such that

\frac{|{p}-\penalty{r_{n}}|-|{p}-\penalty{q}|}{|{q}-\penalty{r_{n}}|}\to\penalty 1.

By the triangle inequality,

|{z}-\penalty{r}|-|{z}-\penalty{q}|,\quad|{z^{\prime}}-\penalty{r}|-|{z^{\prime}}-\penalty{q}|\geqslant\penalty|{p}-\penalty{r}|-|{p}-\penalty{q}|;

thus, as
n\to\penalty\infty
,

\frac{|{z}-\penalty{r_{n}}|-|{z}-\penalty{q}|}{|{q}-\penalty{r_{n}}|},\quad\frac{|{z^{\prime}}-\penalty{r_{n}}|-|{z^{\prime}}-\penalty{q}|}{|{q}-\penalty{r_{n}}|}\to\penalty 1.

Therefore
\text{\hbox to0.0pt{$\tilde{\text{\phantom{n}}}$\hss}{$\measuredangle$}}^{\upkappa}(q\,{}^{z}_{r_{n}}\hskip-0.86108pt)\to\penalty\uppi
and
\text{\hbox to0.0pt{$\tilde{\text{\phantom{n}}}$\hss}{$\measuredangle$}}^{\upkappa}(q\,{}^{z^{\prime}}_{r_{n}}\hskip-0.86108pt)\to\penalty\uppi
.
(Here we use that
t
is small, otherwise if
\upkappa>0
the angles might be undefined.)

From
\text{\rm CBB}({\upkappa})
comparison (
8A
),
\text{\hbox to0.0pt{$\tilde{\text{\phantom{n}}}$\hss}{$\measuredangle$}}^{\upkappa}(q\,{}^{z}_{z^{\prime}}\hskip-0.86108pt)=0
and thus
z=z^{\prime}
.

The proof of existence is similar.
Choose a sequence
r_{n}
as above.
Since
\mathcal{L}
is a complete length space,
there is a sequence
z_{k}\in\penalty\mathcal{L}
such that
|{q}-\penalty{z_{k}}|\to\penalty t
and
|{p}-\penalty{q}|-|{p}-\penalty{z_{k}}|\to\penalty t
as
k\to\penalty\infty
.
Then

\lim_{n\to\penalty\infty}\lim_{k\to\penalty\infty}\text{\hbox to0.0pt{$\tilde{\text{\phantom{n}}}$\hss}{$\measuredangle$}}^{\upkappa}(q\,{}^{z_{k}}_{r_{n}}\hskip-0.86108pt)=\uppi.

Thus, for any
\upvarepsilon>0
and sufficiently large
n,k
, we have
\text{\hbox to0.0pt{$\tilde{\text{\phantom{n}}}$\hss}{$\measuredangle$}}^{\upkappa}(q\,{}^{z_{k}}_{r_{n}}\hskip-0.86108pt)>\penalty\uppi-\upvarepsilon
.
From
\text{\rm CBB}({\upkappa})
comparison (
8A
), for all large
k
and
j
, we have
\text{\hbox to0.0pt{$\tilde{\text{\phantom{n}}}$\hss}{$\measuredangle$}}^{\upkappa}(q\,{}^{z_{k}}_{z_{j}}\hskip-0.86108pt)<\penalty 2{\hskip 0.5pt\cdot\penalty\hskip 0.5pt}\upvarepsilon
and thus

|{z_{k}}-\penalty{z_{j}}|<\upvarepsilon{\hskip 0.5pt\cdot\penalty\hskip 0.5pt}c(\upkappa,t);

that is,
z_{n}
is a Cauchy sequence, and its limit
z
satisfies
➊
.
∎

Let

A\subset\penalty\mathcal{L}

Show that there is a dense G-delta set

A

[xy]

\mathcal{L}
.

C
More comparisons

The following theorem makes it easier to use Euclidean intuition in the Alexandrov
setting.

8.14. Theorem.

If
\mathcal{L}
is a
\text{\rm CBB}({\upkappa})
space,
then the following conditions hold for all
p,x,y\in\penalty\mathcal{L}
, provided the model triangle
{\tilde{\triangle}}^{\upkappa}(pxy)
is defined.

(a)

(adjacent angle comparison
) for any geodesic
[xy]
and
z\in\penalty\mathopen{]}xy\mathclose{[}
,
z\neq p
we have

\text{\hbox to0.0pt{$\tilde{\text{\phantom{n}}}$\hss}{$\measuredangle$}}^{\upkappa}(z\,{}^{p}_{x}\hskip-0.86108pt)+\text{\hbox to0.0pt{$\tilde{\text{\phantom{n}}}$\hss}{$\measuredangle$}}^{\upkappa}(z\,{}^{p}_{y}\hskip-0.86108pt)\leqslant\penalty\uppi.

(b)

(
point-on-side comparison)
for any geodesic
[xy]
and
z\in\penalty\mathopen{]}xy\mathclose{[}
, we have

\text{\hbox to0.0pt{$\tilde{\text{\phantom{n}}}$\hss}{$\measuredangle$}}^{\upkappa}(x\,{}^{p}_{y}\hskip-0.86108pt)\leqslant\penalty\text{\hbox to0.0pt{$\tilde{\text{\phantom{n}}}$\hss}{$\measuredangle$}}^{\upkappa}(x\,{}^{p}_{z}\hskip-0.86108pt);

or, equivalently,

|{\tilde{p}}-\penalty{\tilde{z}}|\leqslant\penalty|{p}-\penalty{z}|,

where
[{\tilde{p}}{\tilde{x}}{\tilde{y}}]={\tilde{\triangle}}^{\upkappa}(pxy)
,
\tilde{z}\in\penalty\mathopen{]}\tilde{x}\tilde{y}\mathclose{[}
,
|{\tilde{x}}-\penalty{\tilde{z}}|=|{x}-\penalty{z}|
.

(c)

(hinge comparison
)

for any hinge
[x\,{}^{p}_{y}]
, the angle

\measuredangle[x\,{}^{p}_{y}]
is defined and

\measuredangle[x\,{}^{p}_{y}]\geqslant\penalty\text{\hbox to0.0pt{$\tilde{\text{\phantom{n}}}$\hss}{$\measuredangle$}}^{\upkappa}(x\,{}^{p}_{y}\hskip-0.86108pt),

or equivalently

\mathop{\tilde{\curlyvee}}\nolimits^{\upkappa}[x\,{}^{p}_{y}]\geqslant\penalty|{p}-\penalty{y}|.

Moreover,

\measuredangle[z\,{}^{p}_{y}]+\measuredangle[z\,{}^{p}_{x}]\leqslant\penalty\uppi

for any two adjacent hinges
[z\,{}^{p}_{y}]
and
[z\,{}^{p}_{x}]
.

Moreover, in each case, the converse holds if
\mathcal{L}
is G-delta geodesic.
That is, if one of the conditions
8C
,
8C
), or
8C
holds in a G-delta geodesic space
\mathcal{L}
, then
\mathcal{L}
is
\text{\rm CBB}({\upkappa})
.

A slightly stronger form of
8C
is given in
8E
.
See also Problem
8I
.

Proof;
8C
).

Since
z\in\penalty\mathopen{]}xy\mathclose{[}
, we have
\text{\hbox to0.0pt{$\tilde{\text{\phantom{n}}}$\hss}{$\measuredangle$}}^{\upkappa}(z\,{}^{x}_{y}\hskip-0.86108pt)=\uppi
.
Thus,
\text{\rm CBB}({\upkappa})
comparison

\text{\hbox to0.0pt{$\tilde{\text{\phantom{n}}}$\hss}{$\measuredangle$}}^{\upkappa}(z\,{}^{x}_{y}\hskip-0.86108pt)+\text{\hbox to0.0pt{$\tilde{\text{\phantom{n}}}$\hss}{$\measuredangle$}}^{\upkappa}(z\,{}^{p}_{x}\hskip-0.86108pt)+\text{\hbox to0.0pt{$\tilde{\text{\phantom{n}}}$\hss}{$\measuredangle$}}^{\upkappa}(z\,{}^{p}_{y}\hskip-0.86108pt)\leqslant\penalty 2{\hskip 0.5pt\cdot\penalty\hskip 0.5pt}\uppi

implies

\text{\hbox to0.0pt{$\tilde{\text{\phantom{n}}}$\hss}{$\measuredangle$}}^{\upkappa}(z\,{}^{p}_{x}\hskip-0.86108pt)+\text{\hbox to0.0pt{$\tilde{\text{\phantom{n}}}$\hss}{$\measuredangle$}}^{\upkappa}(z\,{}^{p}_{y}\hskip-0.86108pt)\leqslant\penalty\uppi.

8C
\Leftrightarrow
8C
.

Follows from Alexandrov’s lemma (
6B
).

8C
+
8C
\Rightarrow
8C
.

From
8C
we get that for
\bar{p}\in\penalty\mathopen{]}xp]
and
\bar{y}\in\penalty\mathopen{]}xy]
, the function
(|{x}-\penalty{\bar{p}}|,|{x}-\penalty{\bar{y}}|)\mapsto\penalty\text{\hbox to0.0pt{$\tilde{\text{\phantom{n}}}$\hss}{$\measuredangle$}}^{\upkappa}(x\,{}^{\bar{p}}_{\bar{y}}\hskip-0.86108pt)
is nonincreasing in each argument.
In particular,

\measuredangle[x\,{}^{p}_{y}]=\penalty\sup\{\text{\hbox to0.0pt{$\tilde{\text{\phantom{n}}}$\hss}{$\measuredangle$}}^{\upkappa}(x\,{}^{\bar{p}}_{\bar{y}}\hskip-0.86108pt)\}

is defined and is
at least
\text{\hbox to0.0pt{$\tilde{\text{\phantom{n}}}$\hss}{$\measuredangle$}}^{\upkappa}(x\,{}^{p}_{y}\hskip-0.86108pt)
.

From above and
8C
, it follows that

\measuredangle[z\,{}^{p}_{y}]+\measuredangle[z\,{}^{p}_{x}]\leqslant\penalty\uppi.

Converse.

Assume first that
\mathcal{L}
is geodesic.
Consider a point
w\in\penalty\mathopen{]}pz\mathclose{[}
close to
p
.
From
8C
, it follows that

\measuredangle[w\,{}^{x}_{z}]+\measuredangle[w\,{}^{x}_{p}]\leqslant\penalty\uppi\quad\text{and}\quad\measuredangle[w\,{}^{y}_{z}]+\measuredangle[w\,{}^{y}_{p}]\leqslant\penalty\uppi.

Since
\measuredangle[w\,{}^{x}_{y}]\leqslant\penalty\measuredangle[w\,{}^{x}_{p}]+\measuredangle[w\,{}^{y}_{p}]
(see
6C
), we get

\measuredangle[w\,{}^{x}_{z}]+\measuredangle[w\,{}^{y}_{z}]+\measuredangle[w\,{}^{x}_{y}]\leqslant\penalty 2{\hskip 0.5pt\cdot\penalty\hskip 0.5pt}\uppi.

Applying the first inequality in
8C
,

\text{\hbox to0.0pt{$\tilde{\text{\phantom{n}}}$\hss}{$\measuredangle$}}^{\upkappa}(w\,{}^{x}_{z}\hskip-0.86108pt)+\text{\hbox to0.0pt{$\tilde{\text{\phantom{n}}}$\hss}{$\measuredangle$}}^{\upkappa}(w\,{}^{y}_{z}\hskip-0.86108pt)+\text{\hbox to0.0pt{$\tilde{\text{\phantom{n}}}$\hss}{$\measuredangle$}}^{\upkappa}(w\,{}^{x}_{y}\hskip-0.86108pt)\leqslant\penalty 2{\hskip 0.5pt\cdot\penalty\hskip 0.5pt}\uppi.

Passing to the limits
w\to\penalty p
, we have

\text{\hbox to0.0pt{$\tilde{\text{\phantom{n}}}$\hss}{$\measuredangle$}}^{\upkappa}(p\,{}^{x}_{z}\hskip-0.86108pt)+\text{\hbox to0.0pt{$\tilde{\text{\phantom{n}}}$\hss}{$\measuredangle$}}^{\upkappa}(p\,{}^{y}_{z}\hskip-0.86108pt)+\text{\hbox to0.0pt{$\tilde{\text{\phantom{n}}}$\hss}{$\measuredangle$}}^{\upkappa}(p\,{}^{x}_{y}\hskip-0.86108pt)\leqslant\penalty 2{\hskip 0.5pt\cdot\penalty\hskip 0.5pt}\uppi.

If
\mathcal{L}
is only G-delta geodesic, we can apply the above arguments to sequences of points
p_{n},w_{n}\to\penalty p
,
x_{n}\to\penalty x
,
y_{n}\to\penalty y
such that
[p_{n}z]
exists,
w_{n}\in\penalty\mathopen{]}zp_{n}\mathclose{[}
and
[x_{n}w_{n}]
,
[y_{n}w_{n}]
exist, and then pass to the limit as
n\to\penalty\infty
.
∎

8.17. Angle-sidelength monotonicity.

Let
p
,

,
y
be points in a complete length
\text{\rm CBB}({\upkappa})

\mathcal{L}
.
Suppose that the model triangle

{\tilde{\triangle}}^{\upkappa}(pxy)
is defined
and there is a geodesic
[xy]
.
Then for
\bar{y}\in\penalty\mathopen{]}xy]
the function

|{x}-\penalty{\bar{y}}|\mapsto\penalty\text{\hbox to0.0pt{$\tilde{\text{\phantom{n}}}$\hss}{$\measuredangle$}}^{\upkappa}(x\,{}^{p}_{\bar{y}}\hskip-0.86108pt)

is nonincreasing.

In particular, if a geodesic
[xp]
exists and
\bar{p}\in\penalty\mathopen{]}xp]

the function

(|{x}-\penalty{\bar{y}}|,|{x}-\penalty{\bar{p}}|)\mapsto\penalty\text{\hbox to0.0pt{$\tilde{\text{\phantom{n}}}$\hss}{$\measuredangle$}}^{\upkappa}(x\,{}^{\bar{p}}_{\bar{y}}\hskip-0.86108pt)

is nonincresing in each argument

(b)

The angle
\measuredangle[x\,{}^{p}_{y}]
is defined and

+

\Rightarrow

of the proof above.

Prove that

Construct a 4-point metric space

\text{\rm CBB}({0})
.

Assume that for a given triangle
[{x^{1}}{x^{2}}{x^{3}}]
in a metric space its
\upkappa
-model triangle

[{\tilde{x}^{1}}{\tilde{x}^{2}}{\tilde{x}^{3}}]={\tilde{\triangle}}^{\upkappa}({x^{1}}{x^{2}}{x^{3}})
is defined.
We say the triangle
[{x^{1}}{x^{2}}{x^{3}}]
is
\upkappa
-thick
if the natural map (see definition
9D
)

[{\tilde{x}^{1}}{\tilde{x}^{2}}{\tilde{x}^{3}}]\to\penalty[{x^{1}}{x^{2}}{x^{3}}]
is distance non contracting.

8.21. Exercise.

Prove that any triangle with perimeter
<\varpi{}^{{\upkappa}}

in a
\text{\rm CBB}({\upkappa})
space is
\upkappa
-thick
.

8.22. Exercise.

(a)

Show that any
\text{\rm CBB}({0})
space
\mathcal{L}
satisfies the following condition:
for any three points
p,q,r\in\penalty\mathcal{L}
, if
\bar{q}
and
\bar{r}
are midpoints of geodesics
[pq]
and
[pr]
respectively, then
2{\hskip 0.5pt\cdot\penalty\hskip 0.5pt}|{\bar{q}}-\penalty{\bar{r}}|\geqslant\penalty|{q}-\penalty{r}|
.

(b)

Show that there is a metric on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
defined by a norm that satisfies the above condition, but is not
\text{\rm CBB}({0})
.

Remarks.

Monotonicity of the model angle with respect to adjacent sidelengths (
8C
) was named the
convexity property
by Alexandrov.

D
Function comparison

In this section we will translate the angle comparison definitions (Theorem
8C
)
to a concavity-like property of the distance functions as defined in Section
3F
.
This is a conceptual step —
we reformulate a global geometric condition into an infinitesimal condition on distance functions.

8.23. Theorem.

Let
\mathcal{L}
be a complete length space.
Then the following
statements are equivalent:

(a)

\mathcal{L}
is
\text{\rm CBB}({\upkappa})
.

(b)

(function comparison
)
\mathcal{L}
is G-delta geodesic and for any
p\in\penalty\mathcal{L}
, the function
f=\mathrm{md}^{\upkappa}\circ\penalty\mathop{\rm dist}\nolimits_{p}{}{}
satisfies the differential inequality

f^{\prime\prime}\leqslant\penalty 1-\upkappa{\hskip 0.5pt\cdot\penalty\hskip 0.5pt}f.

in
\mathrm{B}{}(p,\varpi{}^{{\upkappa}})
.

8.24. Corollary.

A complete G-delta geodesic space
\mathcal{L}
is
\text{\rm CBB}({0})
if and only if for any
p\in\penalty\mathcal{L}
, the function
\mathop{\rm dist}\nolimits_{p}^{2}{}{}\colon\mathcal{L}\to\penalty\mathbb{R}

is
2
-concave.

Proof of
8D
.

Let
[xy]
be a geodesic in
\mathrm{B}{}(p,\varpi{}^{{\upkappa}})
and
\ell=|{x}-\penalty{y}|
.
Consider the model triangle
[{\tilde{p}}{\tilde{x}}{\tilde{y}}]={\tilde{\triangle}}^{\upkappa}(pxy)
.
Set

\displaystyle\tilde{r}(t)

\displaystyle=|{\tilde{p}}-\penalty{\mathrm{geod}_{[\tilde{x}\tilde{y}]}(t)}|,

\displaystyle r(t)

\displaystyle=|{p}-\penalty{\mathrm{geod}_{[xy]}(t)}|.

Clearly
\tilde{r}(0)=r(0)
and
\tilde{r}(\ell)=r(\ell)
.
Set
\tilde{f}=\mathrm{md}^{\upkappa}\circ\penalty\tilde{r}
and
f=\mathrm{md}^{\upkappa}\circ\penalty r
.
From
1A
we get that
\tilde{f}^{\prime\prime}=1-\upkappa{\hskip 0.5pt\cdot\penalty\hskip 0.5pt}\tilde{f}
.

Note that the point-on-side comparison (
8C
) for point
p
and geodesic
[xy]
is equivalent to
\tilde{r}\leqslant\penalty r
.
Since
\mathrm{md}^{\upkappa}
is increasing on
[0,\varpi{}^{{\upkappa}})
,

\tilde{r}\leqslant\penalty r
is equivalent to
\tilde{f}\leqslant\penalty f
.
The latter is Jensen’s inequality (
3E
) for the function

t\mapsto\penalty\mathrm{md}^{\upkappa}|{p}-\penalty{\mathrm{geod}_{[xy]}(t)}|
on the interval
[0,\ell]
.
Hence the result.
∎

Recall that Busemann functions are defined in Proposition
6A
.

8.25. Exercise.

Let
\mathcal{L}
be a complete length
\text{\rm CBB}({\upkappa})
space
and
\mathop{\rm bus}\nolimits_{\upgamma}\colon\mathcal{L}\to\penalty\mathbb{R}
be the Busemann function for a half-line
\upgamma\colon[0,\infty)\to\penalty\mathcal{L}
.

(a)

If
\upkappa=0
, then the Busemann function
\mathop{\rm bus}\nolimits_{\upgamma}
is concave.

(b)

If
\upkappa=-1
, then the function

f=\exp\circ\penalty\mathop{\rm bus}\nolimits_{\upgamma}

satisfies

f^{\prime\prime}-f\leqslant\penalty 0.

Exercise
9E
is an analogous statement for upper curvature bound.

E
Development

In this section we reformulate the function comparison using a more geometric language based on the definition of development given below.

This definition appears in
[
17
]

and an earlier form of it can be found in
[
104
]
.
The definition is somewhat lengthy, but it defines a useful comparison object for a curve.
Often it is easier to write proofs in terms of function comparison but think in terms of developments.

8.26. Lemma-definition.

Let
\upkappa\in\penalty\mathbb{R}
,

\mathcal{X}
be a metric space,

\upgamma\colon\mathbb{I}\to\penalty\mathcal{X}
be a
1
-Lipschitz curve,

p\in\penalty\mathcal{X}
,
and
\tilde{p}\in\penalty\mathbb{M}^{2}({\upkappa})
.
Assume
0<|{p}-\penalty{\upgamma(t)}|<\varpi{}^{{\upkappa}}
for
all
t\in\penalty\mathbb{I}
.
Then there exists a unique up to rotation curve

\tilde{\upgamma}\colon\mathbb{I}\to\penalty\mathbb{M}^{2}({\upkappa})
, parametrized by arc-length,
such that

|{\tilde{p}}-\penalty{\tilde{\upgamma}(t)}|=\penalty|{p}-\penalty{\upgamma(t)}|
for all
t

and the direction of

[\tilde{p}\tilde{\upgamma}(t)]
monotonically turns around
\tilde{p}
counterclockwise as
t
increases.

If
p
,
\tilde{p}
,
\upgamma
, and
\tilde{\upgamma}
are as above,
then
\tilde{\upgamma}
is called the
\upkappa
-development
of
\upgamma
with respect to
p
;
the point
\tilde{p}
is called the
basepoint
of the development.
When we say that the
\upkappa
-development of
\upgamma
with respect to
p
is defined, we always assume that
0<|{p}-\penalty{\upgamma(t)}|<\varpi{}^{{\upkappa}}
for
all

t\in\penalty\mathbb{I}
.

Proof.

Consider the functions
\uprho
,
\upvartheta\colon\mathbb{I}\to\penalty\mathbb{R}
defined as

\displaystyle\uprho(t)

\displaystyle=|{p}-\penalty{\upgamma(t)}|,

\displaystyle\upvartheta(t)

\displaystyle=\int\limits_{t_{0}}^{t}\frac{\sqrt{1-(\uprho^{\prime})^{2}}}{\mathrm{sn}^{\upkappa}\uprho},

where
t_{0}\in\penalty\mathbb{I}
is a fixed number and
\int
denotes Lebesgue integral.
Since
\upgamma
is
1
-Lipshitz, so is
\uprho(t)
, and thus the function
\upvartheta
is defined and nondecreasing.

It is straightforward to check that
(\uprho,\upvartheta)
uniquely describe
\tilde{\upgamma}
in polar coordinates on
\mathbb{M}^{2}({\upkappa})
with center at
\tilde{p}
.
∎

We need the following analogs of sub- and super-graphs and convex/concave functions, adapted to polar coordinates in
\mathbb{M}^{2}({\upkappa})
.

8.27. Definition.

Let
\tilde{\upgamma}\colon\mathbb{I}\to\penalty\mathbb{M}^{2}({\upkappa})
be a curve and
\tilde{p}\in\penalty\mathbb{M}^{2}({\upkappa})
be such that there is a unique geodesic
[\tilde{p}\,\tilde{\upgamma}(t)]
for any
t\in\penalty\mathbb{I}
and the direction of
[\tilde{p}\,\tilde{\upgamma}(t)]
turns monotonically as
t
grows.

The set formed by all geodesics from
\tilde{p}
to the points on
\tilde{\upgamma}
is called the
subgraph
of
\tilde{\upgamma}
with respect to
\tilde{p}
.

The set of all points
\tilde{x}\in\penalty\mathbb{M}^{2}({\upkappa})
such that a geodesic
[\tilde{p}\tilde{x}]
intersects
\tilde{\upgamma}
is called the
supergraph
of
\tilde{\upgamma}
with respect to
\tilde{p}
.

The curve
\tilde{\upgamma}
is called
convex (concave) with respect to

\tilde{p}
if the subgraph (supergraph) of
\tilde{\upgamma}
with respect to
\tilde{p}
is convex.

The curve
\tilde{\upgamma}
is called

locally convex (concave) with respect to
\tilde{p}

if for any interior value
t_{0}
in
\mathbb{I}
there is a subsegment
(a,b)\subset\penalty\mathbb{I}
,
(a,b)\ni\penalty t_{0}
, such that the restriction
\tilde{\upgamma}|_{(a,b)}
is convex (concave) with respect to
\tilde{p}
.

Note that if
\upkappa>0
, then the supergraph of a curve is the subgraph with respect to the opposite point.

For developments,
all the notions above will be considered with respect to their basepoints.
In particular, if
\tilde{\upgamma}
is a development, we will say it is
(locally) convex
if it is (locally) convex with respect to its basepoint.

8.28. Development comparison.

A complete G-delta geodesic space

\mathcal{L}
is
\text{\rm CBB}({\upkappa})
if and only if for any point
p\in\penalty\mathcal{L}
and any geodesic
\upgamma
in
\mathrm{B}{}(p,\varpi{}^{{\upkappa}})\setminus\{p\}
,
its
\upkappa
-development with respect to
p
is convex.

A simpler proof of the only-if part can be built on the adjacent angle comparison (
8C
).
We use a longer proof since it also implies the short hinge lemma (
8E
).

Proof; only-if part.

Let
\upgamma\colon[0,T]\to\penalty\mathrm{B}{}(p,\varpi{}^{{\upkappa}})\setminus\{p\}
be a unit-speed geodesic in
\mathcal{L}
.

Consider a fine partition

0=t_{0}<t_{1}<\dots\penalty<t_{n}=T.

Set
x_{i}=\upgamma(t_{i})
and choose a point

p^{\prime}\in\penalty\mathop{\rm Str}\nolimits(x_{0},x_{1},\dots\penalty,x_{n})

sufficiently close to
p
;
recall that geodesics
[p^{\prime}x_{i}]
exist for all
i

(see
8B
).

Let us construct a chain of model triangles

[{\tilde{p}^{\prime}}{\tilde{x}_{i-1}}{\tilde{x}_{i}}]=\penalty{\tilde{\triangle}}^{\upkappa}(p^{\prime}x_{i-1}x_{i})
in such a way that direction
[\tilde{p}^{\prime}\tilde{x}_{i}]
turns counterclockwise as
i
grows.
By the hinge comparison (
8C
), we have

\displaystyle\measuredangle[\tilde{x}_{i}\,{}^{\tilde{x}_{i-1}}_{\tilde{p}^{\prime}}]+\measuredangle[\tilde{x}_{i}\,{}^{\tilde{x}_{i+1}}_{\tilde{p}^{\prime}}]

\displaystyle=\text{\hbox to0.0pt{$\tilde{\text{\phantom{n}}}$\hss}{$\measuredangle$}}^{\upkappa}(x_{i}\,{}^{x_{i-1}}_{p^{\prime}}\hskip-0.86108pt)+\text{\hbox to0.0pt{$\tilde{\text{\phantom{n}}}$\hss}{$\measuredangle$}}^{\upkappa}(x_{i}\,{}^{x_{i+1}}_{p^{\prime}}\hskip-0.86108pt)\leqslant\penalty

➊

\displaystyle\leqslant\penalty\measuredangle[x_{i}\,{}^{x_{i-1}}_{p^{\prime}}]+\measuredangle[x_{i}\,{}^{x_{i+1}}_{p^{\prime}}]\leqslant\penalty

\displaystyle\leqslant\penalty\uppi.

Further, since
\upgamma
is a unit-speed geodesic, we have

\sum_{i=1}^{n}|{x_{i-1}}-\penalty{x_{i}}|\leqslant\penalty|{p^{\prime}}-\penalty{x_{0}}|+|{p^{\prime}}-\penalty{x_{n}}|.

➋

Since
p^{\prime}\notin\upgamma
, the development comparison implies that
\tilde{p}^{\prime}
does not lie on the polygonal line
\tilde{x}_{0}\dots\penalty\tilde{x}_{n}
.

If
\upkappa\leqslant\penalty 0
, then
➋
implies that

\upvartheta:=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\measuredangle[\tilde{p}^{\prime}\,{}^{\tilde{x}_{i}}_{\tilde{x}_{i-1}}]\leqslant\penalty\uppi.

➌

In the case
\upkappa>0
, the proof of
➌
requires more work.
Applying rescaling, we can assume that
\upkappa=1
.
Since
\upgamma
lies in
B_{\uppi}(p^{\prime})
, the point-on-side comparison implies that the antipodal point of
\tilde{p}^{\prime}
does not lie on the polygonal line
\tilde{x}_{0}\dots\penalty\tilde{x}_{n}
.

Consider the space
L
glued from the solid model triangles
[{\tilde{p}^{\prime}}{\tilde{x}_{0}}{\tilde{x}_{1}}],\dots\penalty\penalty,[{\tilde{p}^{\prime}}{\tilde{x}_{n-1}}{\tilde{x}_{n}}]
along the corresponding sides.
Note that
\upvartheta
is the total angle of
L
at
\tilde{p}^{\prime}
.
We can assume that
L
has nonempty interior.
Otherwise all the triangles are degenerate, and therefore
\upvartheta=0
;
the latter holds since
\tilde{p}^{\prime}
is not on the polygonal line
\tilde{x}_{0}\dots\penalty\tilde{x}_{n}
.

Consider a minimizing geodesic
[\tilde{x}_{0}\tilde{x}_{n}]_{L}
.
By
➋
we may assume that
\tilde{p}^{\prime}\notin[\tilde{x}_{0}\tilde{x}_{n}]_{L}
.
Further if the geodesic
[\tilde{x}_{0}\tilde{x}_{n}]_{L}
contains one of the points

\tilde{x}_{1},\dots\penalty,\tilde{x}_{n-1}
, then it coincides with the polygonal line
\tilde{x}_{0}\dots\penalty\tilde{x}_{n}
.
(In particular, we have equality in
➊
for each
i
.)
In this case, the sum in the left-hand side of
➋
must be at most
\uppi
; otherwise
[\tilde{x}_{0}\tilde{x}_{n}]_{L}
is not minimizing.
Therefore
➌
follows.
In the remaining case
[\tilde{x}_{0}\tilde{x}_{n}]_{L}
meets the boundary of
L
only at its ends.
In this case,
|{\tilde{x}_{0}}-\penalty{\tilde{x}_{n}}|_{L}\leqslant\penalty\uppi
; otherwise
[\tilde{x}_{0}\tilde{x}_{n}]_{L}
is not minimizing.
Whence
➌
follows.

Inequalities
➊
and
➌
imply that the polygon
[\tilde{p}^{\prime}\tilde{x}_{0}\tilde{x}_{1}\dots\penalty\tilde{x}_{n}]
is convex.

Let us take finer and finer partitions and pass to the limit of the polygon
\tilde{p}^{\prime}\tilde{x}_{0}\tilde{x}_{1}\dots\penalty\tilde{x}_{n}
as
p^{\prime}\to\penalty p
.
We obtain a convex curvilinear triangle formed by a curve
\tilde{\upgamma}\colon[0,T]\to\penalty\mathbb{M}^{2}({\upkappa})
— the limit of polygonal line
\tilde{x}_{0}\tilde{x}_{1}\dots\penalty\tilde{x}_{n}

and two geodesics
[\tilde{p}^{\prime}\,\tilde{\upgamma}(0)]
,

[\tilde{p}^{\prime}\,\tilde{\upgamma}(T)]
.
Since
[\tilde{p}^{\prime}\tilde{x}_{0}\tilde{x}_{1}\dots\penalty\tilde{x}_{n}]
is convex,
the natural parametrization of
\tilde{x}_{0}\tilde{x}_{1}\dots\penalty\tilde{x}_{n}

converges to the natural parametrization of
\tilde{\upgamma}
. Thus
\tilde{\upgamma}
is the
\upkappa
-development of
\upgamma
with respect to
p
.
This proves the only-if part of (
8E
).

If part.

Assuming convexity of the development, we will prove the point-on-side comparison (
8C
).
We can assume that
p\notin[xy]
; otherwise the statement is trivial.

Set
T=|{x}-\penalty{y}|
and
\upgamma(t)=\mathrm{geod}_{[xy]}(t)
; note that
\upgamma
is a geodesic in
\mathrm{B}{}(p,\varpi{}^{{\upkappa}})\setminus\{p\}
.
Let
\tilde{\upgamma}\colon[0,T]\to\penalty\mathbb{M}^{2}({\upkappa})
be the
\upkappa
-development with base
\tilde{p}
of
\upgamma
with respect to
p
.
Take a partition
0=t_{0}<t_{1}<\dots\penalty<t_{n}=T
, and set

\tilde{y}_{i}=\tilde{\upgamma}(t_{i})\quad\text{and}\quad\uptau_{i}=|{\tilde{y}_{0}}-\penalty{\tilde{y}_{1}}|+|{\tilde{y}_{1}}-\penalty{\tilde{y}_{2}}|+\dots\penalty+|{\tilde{y}_{i-1}}-\penalty{\tilde{y}_{i}}|.

Since
\tilde{\upgamma}
is convex, for a fine partition we have that the polygonal line
\tilde{y}_{0}\tilde{y}_{1}\dots\penalty\tilde{y}_{n}
is also convex.
Applying Alexandrov’s lemma (
6B
) inductively to pairs of model triangles

{\tilde{\triangle}}^{\upkappa}\{\uptau_{i-1},|{\tilde{p}}-\penalty{\tilde{y}_{0}}|,|{\tilde{p}}-\penalty{\tilde{y}_{i-1}}|\}

and

{\tilde{\triangle}}^{\upkappa}\{|{\tilde{y}_{i-1}}-\penalty{\tilde{y}_{i}}|,|{\tilde{p}}-\penalty{\tilde{y}_{i-1}}|,|{\tilde{p}}-\penalty{\tilde{y}_{i}}|\}

we obtain that the sequence

\tilde{\measuredangle}^{\upkappa}\{|{\tilde{p}}-\penalty{\tilde{y}_{i}}|;|{\tilde{p}}-\penalty{\tilde{y}_{0}}|,\uptau_{i}\}
is non increasing.

For finer and finer partitions we have

\max\nolimits_{i}\{|\uptau_{i}-t_{i}|\}\to\penalty 0.

Thus, the point-on-side comparison (
8C
) follows.
∎

Note that in the proof of if part we could use a slightly weaker version of the hinge comparison (
8C
).
Namely, we proved the following lemma, which will be needed later in the proof of the globalization theorem (
8F
).

8.29. Short hinge lemma.

Let
\mathcal{L}
be a complete G-delta geodesic space such that for any hinge
[x\,{}^{p}_{y}]
in
\mathcal{L}
the angle
\measuredangle[x\,{}^{p}_{y}]
is defined,
and

\measuredangle[x\,{}^{p}_{y}]+\measuredangle[x\,{}^{p}_{z}]\leqslant\penalty\uppi

for any two adjacent hinges.

Assume that for any hinge
[x\,{}^{p}_{y}]
in
\mathcal{L}
we have

|{p}-\penalty{x}|+|{x}-\penalty{y}|<\varpi{}^{{\upkappa}}\quad\Rightarrow\quad\measuredangle[x\,{}^{p}_{y}]\geqslant\penalty\text{\hbox to0.0pt{$\tilde{\text{\phantom{n}}}$\hss}{$\measuredangle$}}^{\upkappa}(x\,{}^{p}_{y}\hskip-0.86108pt).

Then
\mathcal{L}
is
\text{\rm CBB}({\upkappa})
.

F
Local definitions and globalization

In this section we discuss locally
\text{\rm CBB}({\upkappa})
spaces.
In particular, we prove the
globalization theorem
: equivalence of local and global definitions for complete length spaces.

The following theorem summarizes equivalent definitions of locally
\text{\rm CBB}({\upkappa})
spaces

8.30. Theorem.

Let
\mathcal{X}
be a complete length space and
p\in\penalty\mathcal{X}
.
Then the following conditions are equivalent:

1)

(local
\text{\rm CBB}({\upkappa})
comparison) there is
R_{\hbox{\rm\scriptsize\ref{SHORT.curv>=k}}}>0
such that the comparison

\text{\hbox to0.0pt{$\tilde{\text{\phantom{n}}}$\hss}{$\measuredangle$}}^{\upkappa}(q\,{}^{x^{1}}_{x^{2}}\hskip-0.86108pt)+\text{\hbox to0.0pt{$\tilde{\text{\phantom{n}}}$\hss}{$\measuredangle$}}^{\upkappa}(q\,{}^{x^{2}}_{x^{3}}\hskip-0.86108pt)+\text{\hbox to0.0pt{$\tilde{\text{\phantom{n}}}$\hss}{$\measuredangle$}}^{\upkappa}(q\,{}^{x^{3}}_{x^{1}}\hskip-0.86108pt)\leqslant\penalty 2{\hskip 0.5pt\cdot\penalty\hskip 0.5pt}\uppi

holds for any
q,x^{1},x^{2},x^{3}\in\penalty\mathrm{B}{}(p,R_{\hbox{\rm\scriptsize\ref{SHORT.curv>=k}}})
.

2)

(local Kirszbraun property)
there is
R_{\hbox{\rm\scriptsize\ref{SHORT.def:kirszbraun-loc}}}>0

such that for any 3-point subset
F_{3}
and any 4-point subset
F_{4}\supset\penalty F_{3}
in
\mathrm{B}{}(p,R_{\hbox{\rm\scriptsize\ref{SHORT.def:kirszbraun-loc}}})
, any short map
f\colon F_{3}\to\penalty\mathbb{M}^{2}({\upkappa})
can be extended to a short map
\bar{f}\colon F_{4}\to\penalty\mathbb{M}^{2}({\upkappa})
(so
f=\bar{f}|_{F_{3}}
).

3)

(local function comparison) there is

R_{\hbox{\rm\scriptsize\ref{SHORT.conc-loc}}}>0
such that

\mathrm{B}{}(p,R_{\hbox{\rm\scriptsize\ref{SHORT.conc-loc}}})

is G-delta geodesic and for any

q\in\penalty\mathrm{B}{}(p,R_{\hbox{\rm\scriptsize\ref{SHORT.conc-loc}}})
,
the function
f=\mathrm{md}^{\upkappa}\circ\penalty\mathop{\rm dist}\nolimits_{q}{}{}
satisfies

f^{\prime\prime}\leqslant\penalty 1-\upkappa{\hskip 0.5pt\cdot\penalty\hskip 0.5pt}f
in

\mathrm{B}{}(p,R_{\hbox{\rm\scriptsize\ref{SHORT.conc-loc}}})
.

4)

(local adjacent angle comparison) there is

R_{\hbox{\rm\scriptsize\ref{SHORT.2-sum-loc}}}>0

such that
\mathrm{B}{}(p,R_{\hbox{\rm\scriptsize\ref{SHORT.2-sum-loc}}})

is G-delta geodesic, and if
q
and a geodesic
[xy]
lie in

\mathrm{B}{}(p,R_{\hbox{\rm\scriptsize\ref{SHORT.2-sum-loc}}})

and
z\in\penalty\mathopen{]}xy\mathclose{[}
, then

\text{\hbox to0.0pt{$\tilde{\text{\phantom{n}}}$\hss}{$\measuredangle$}}^{\upkappa}(z\,{}^{q}_{x}\hskip-0.86108pt)+\text{\hbox to0.0pt{$\tilde{\text{\phantom{n}}}$\hss}{$\measuredangle$}}^{\upkappa}(z\,{}^{q}_{y}\hskip-0.86108pt)\leqslant\penalty\uppi.

5)

(local point-on-side comparison)
there is
R_{\hbox{\rm\scriptsize\ref{SHORT.monoton-loc}}}>0

such that
\mathrm{B}{}(p,R_{\hbox{\rm\scriptsize\ref{SHORT.monoton-loc}}})
is G-delta geodesic and if
q
and a geodesic
[xy]
lie in
\mathrm{B}{}(p,R_{\hbox{\rm\scriptsize\ref{SHORT.monoton-loc}}})

and
z\in\penalty\mathopen{]}xy\mathclose{[}
, then

\text{\hbox to0.0pt{$\tilde{\text{\phantom{n}}}$\hss}{$\measuredangle$}}^{\upkappa}(x\,{}^{q}_{y}\hskip-0.86108pt)\leqslant\penalty\text{\hbox to0.0pt{$\tilde{\text{\phantom{n}}}$\hss}{$\measuredangle$}}^{\upkappa}(x\,{}^{q}_{z}\hskip-0.86108pt);

or, equivalently,

|{\tilde{p}}-\penalty{\tilde{z}}|\leqslant\penalty|{p}-\penalty{z}|,

where
[{\tilde{p}}{\tilde{x}}{\tilde{y}}]={\tilde{\triangle}}^{\upkappa}(pxy)
,
\tilde{z}\in\penalty\mathopen{]}\tilde{x}\tilde{y}\mathclose{[}
,
|{\tilde{x}}-\penalty{\tilde{z}}|=|{x}-\penalty{z}|
.

6)

(local hinge comparison)
there is
R_{\hbox{\rm\scriptsize\ref{SHORT.angle-loc}}}>0
such that
\mathrm{B}{}(p,R_{\hbox{\rm\scriptsize\ref{SHORT.angle-loc}}})
is G-delta geodesic and if
x\in\penalty\mathrm{B}{}(p,R_{\hbox{\rm\scriptsize\ref{SHORT.angle-loc}}})
, then
for any hinge
[x\,{}^{q}_{y}]
, the angle

\measuredangle[x\,{}^{q}_{y}]
is defined, and

\measuredangle[x\,{}^{q}_{y}]+\measuredangle[x\,{}^{q}_{z}]\leqslant\penalty\uppi

for any two adjacent hinges.
Moreover, if a hinge
[x\,{}^{q}_{y}]
lies in
\mathrm{B}{}(p,R_{\hbox{\rm\scriptsize\ref{SHORT.angle-loc}}})
, then

\measuredangle[x\,{}^{q}_{y}]\geqslant\penalty\text{\hbox to0.0pt{$\tilde{\text{\phantom{n}}}$\hss}{$\measuredangle$}}^{\upkappa}(x\,{}^{q}_{y}\hskip-0.86108pt),

or, equivalently,

\mathop{\tilde{\curlyvee}}\nolimits^{\upkappa}[x\,{}^{q}_{y}]\geqslant\penalty|{q}-\penalty{y}|.

7)

(local development comparison)
there is
R_{\hbox{\rm\scriptsize\ref{SHORT.def:devel-alex-loc}}}>0

such that
\mathrm{B}{}(p,R_{\hbox{\rm\scriptsize\ref{SHORT.def:devel-alex-loc}}})

is G-delta geodesic, and if a geodesic
\upgamma
lies in
\mathrm{B}{}(p,R_{\hbox{\rm\scriptsize\ref{SHORT.def:devel-alex-loc}}})
and
q\in\penalty\mathrm{B}{}(p,R_{\hbox{\rm\scriptsize\ref{SHORT.def:devel-alex-loc}}})\setminus\upgamma
, then the
\upkappa
-development
\tilde{\upgamma}
with respect to
q
is convex.

Moreover, for each pair
i,j\in\penalty\{1,2,\dots\penalty,7\}
we can assume that

R_{i}>\tfrac{1}{9}{\hskip 0.5pt\cdot\penalty\hskip 0.5pt}R_{j}.

The proofs of each of these equivalences repeat the proofs of the corresponding global equivalences in localized form; see the proofs of Theorems
8C
,
8D
,
8E
,
10A
.

8.31. Globalization theorem.

Any complete length locally
\text{\rm CBB}({\upkappa})
space is
\text{\rm CBB}({\upkappa})
.

In the two-dimensional case this theorem was proved by Paolo Pizzetti
[
133
]
;
later it was reproved independently by Alexandr Alexandrov
[
17
]
. Victor Toponogov
[
153
]
proved it for Riemannian manifolds of all dimensions.
In the above generality, the theorem first appears in the paper of Michael Gromov, Yuriy Burago, and Grigory Perelman
[
43
]
;
simplifications and modifications were given by Conrad Plaut
[
135
]
, Katsuhiro Shiohama
[
147
]
, and in the book of Dmitry Burago, Yuriy Burago, and Sergei Ivanov
[
36
]
.
A generalization for non-complete but geodesic spaces was obtained by the third author
[
126
]
; namely it solves the following exercise:

8.32. Advanced exercise.

Any locally
\text{\rm CBB}({\upkappa})
geodesic space is
\text{\rm CBB}({\upkappa})
.

The following corollary of the globalization theorem says that the expression “space with curvature
\geqslant\penalty\upkappa
” makes sense.

8.33. Corollary.

Let
\mathcal{L}
be a complete length space.
Then
\mathcal{L}
is
\text{\rm CBB}({\mathrm{K}})
if and only if
\mathcal{L}
is
\text{\rm CBB}({\upkappa})
for any
\upkappa<\mathrm{K}
.

Proof.

Note that if
\mathrm{K}\leqslant\penalty 0
, this statement follows directly from the definition of an Alexandrov space (
8A
) and monotonicity of the function
\upkappa\mapsto\penalty\text{\hbox to0.0pt{$\tilde{\text{\phantom{n}}}$\hss}{$\measuredangle$}}^{\upkappa}(x\,{}^{y}_{z}\hskip-0.86108pt)
(
1A
).

The if part also follows directly from the definition.

For
\mathrm{K}>0
, the angle
\text{\hbox to0.0pt{$\tilde{\text{\phantom{n}}}$\hss}{$\measuredangle$}}^{\mathrm{K}}(x\,{}^{y}_{z}\hskip-0.86108pt)
might be undefined while
\text{\hbox to0.0pt{$\tilde{\text{\phantom{n}}}$\hss}{$\measuredangle$}}^{\upkappa}(x\,{}^{y}_{z}\hskip-0.86108pt)
is defined.
However,
\text{\hbox to0.0pt{$\tilde{\text{\phantom{n}}}$\hss}{$\measuredangle$}}^{\mathrm{K}}(x\,{}^{y}_{z}\hskip-0.86108pt)
is defined if
x
,
y
, and
z
are sufficiently close to each other.
Thus, if
\mathrm{K}>\upkappa
, then any
\text{\rm CBB}({\mathrm{K}})
space is locally
\text{\rm CBB}({\upkappa})
.
It remains to apply the globalization theorem.
∎

8.34. Corollary.

Let
\mathcal{L}
be a complete length
\text{\rm CBB}({\upkappa})
space.
Assume that a space
\mathcal{M}
is the target space of a submetry from
\mathcal{L}
.
Then
\mathcal{M}
is a complete length space
\text{\rm CBB}({\upkappa})
space.

In particular, if
G\curvearrowright\mathcal{L}
is an isometric group action with closed orbits, then the quotient space
\mathcal{L}/G
is a complete length
\text{\rm CBB}({\upkappa})
space.

Proof.

This follows from the globalization theorem and Theorem
8A
.
∎

Our proof of the globalization theorem (
8F
) is based on presentations in
[
135
]
and
[
36
]
;
this proof was rediscovered independently by Urs Lang and Viktor Schroeder
[
98
]
.
We will need the short hinge lemma (
8E
) together with the following two lemmas.
The following lemma says that if comparison holds for all small hinges, then it holds for slightly bigger hinges near the given point.

8.35. Key lemma.

Let
\upkappa\in\penalty\mathbb{R}
,

0<\ell\leqslant\penalty\varpi{}^{{\upkappa}}
,

\mathcal{X}
be a complete geodesic space
and
p\in\penalty\mathcal{X}
be a point
such that
\mathrm{B}{}(p,2{\hskip 0.5pt\cdot\penalty\hskip 0.5pt}\ell)
is locally
\text{\rm CBB}({\upkappa})
.

Assume that for any point

q\in\penalty\mathrm{B}{}(p,{\ell})
the comparison

\measuredangle[x\,{}^{y}_{q}]\geqslant\penalty\text{\hbox to0.0pt{$\tilde{\text{\phantom{n}}}$\hss}{$\measuredangle$}}^{\upkappa}(x\,{}^{y}_{q}\hskip-0.86108pt)

holds for any hinge
[x\,{}^{y}_{q}]
with

|{x}-\penalty{y}|+|{x}-\penalty{q}|<\frac{2}{3}{\hskip 0.5pt\cdot\penalty\hskip 0.5pt}\ell
.
Then the comparison

\measuredangle[x\,{}^{p}_{q}]\geqslant\penalty\text{\hbox to0.0pt{$\tilde{\text{\phantom{n}}}$\hss}{$\measuredangle$}}^{\upkappa}(x\,{}^{p}_{q}\hskip-0.86108pt)

holds for any hinge
[x\,{}^{p}_{q}]
with
|{x}-\penalty{p}|+|{x}-\penalty{q}|<\ell
.

Proof.

It is sufficient to prove the inequality

\mathop{\tilde{\curlyvee}}\nolimits^{\upkappa}[x\,{}^{p}_{q}]\geqslant\penalty|{p}-\penalty{q}|

➊

for any hinge
[x\,{}^{p}_{q}]
with
|{x}-\penalty{p}|+|{x}-\penalty{q}|<\ell
.

Fix
q
.
Consider a hinge
[x\,{}^{p}_{q}]
such that

\tfrac{2}{3}{\hskip 0.5pt\cdot\penalty\hskip 0.5pt}\ell\leqslant\penalty|{p}-\penalty{x}|+\penalty|{x}-\penalty{q}|<\ell.

First we construct a new smaller hinge
[x^{\prime}\,{}^{p}_{q}]
with

|{p}-\penalty{x}|+|{x}-\penalty{q}|\geqslant\penalty|{p}-\penalty{x^{\prime}}|+|{x^{\prime}}-\penalty{q}|,

➋

such that

\mathop{\tilde{\curlyvee}}\nolimits^{\upkappa}[x\,{}^{p}_{q}]\geqslant\penalty\mathop{\tilde{\curlyvee}}\nolimits^{\upkappa}[x^{\prime}\,{}^{p}_{q}].

➌

Assume
|{x}-\penalty{q}|\geqslant\penalty|{x}-\penalty{p}|
; otherwise switch the roles of
p
and
q
in the following construction.
Take
x^{\prime}\in\penalty[xq]
such that

|{p}-\penalty{x}|+3{\hskip 0.5pt\cdot\penalty\hskip 0.5pt}|{x}-\penalty{x^{\prime}}|=\tfrac{2}{3}{\hskip 0.5pt\cdot\penalty\hskip 0.5pt}\ell.

➍

Choose a geodesic
[x^{\prime}p]
and consider the hinge
[x^{\prime}\,{}^{p}_{q}]
formed by
[x^{\prime}p]
and
[x^{\prime}q]\subset\penalty[xq]
.
(In fact by
8G
the condition
[x^{\prime}q]\subset\penalty[xq]
always holds.)
Then
➋
follows from the triangle inequality.

Further, note that we have
x,x^{\prime}\in\penalty\mathrm{B}{}(p,\ell)\cap\penalty\mathrm{B}{}(q,\ell)
and moreover

\displaystyle|{p}-\penalty{x}|+\penalty|{x}-\penalty{x^{\prime}}|

\displaystyle<\tfrac{2}{3}{\hskip 0.5pt\cdot\penalty\hskip 0.5pt}\ell,

\displaystyle|{p}-\penalty{x^{\prime}}|+\penalty|{x^{\prime}}-\penalty{x}|

\displaystyle<\tfrac{2}{3}{\hskip 0.5pt\cdot\penalty\hskip 0.5pt}\ell.

In particular,

\measuredangle[x\,{}^{p}_{x^{\prime}}]\geqslant\penalty\text{\hbox to0.0pt{$\tilde{\text{\phantom{n}}}$\hss}{$\measuredangle$}}^{\upkappa}(x\,{}^{p}_{x^{\prime}}\hskip-0.86108pt)\quad\text{and}\quad\measuredangle[x^{\prime}\,{}^{p}_{x}]\geqslant\penalty\text{\hbox to0.0pt{$\tilde{\text{\phantom{n}}}$\hss}{$\measuredangle$}}^{\upkappa}(x^{\prime}\,{}^{p}_{x}\hskip-0.86108pt).

➎

Now, let

[{\tilde{x}}{\tilde{x}^{\prime}}{\tilde{p}}]={\tilde{\triangle}}^{\upkappa}(xx^{\prime}p)
.
Take
\tilde{q}
on the extension of
[\tilde{x}\tilde{x}^{\prime}]
beyond
x^{\prime}
such that
|{\tilde{x}}-\penalty{\tilde{q}}|=|{x}-\penalty{q}|
(and therefore
|{\tilde{x}^{\prime}}-\penalty{\tilde{q}}|=|{x^{\prime}}-\penalty{q}|
).
From
➎
,

\measuredangle[x\,{}^{p}_{q}]=\measuredangle[x\,{}^{p}_{x^{\prime}}]\geqslant\penalty\text{\hbox to0.0pt{$\tilde{\text{\phantom{n}}}$\hss}{$\measuredangle$}}^{\upkappa}(x\,{}^{p}_{x^{\prime}}\hskip-0.86108pt)\quad\Rightarrow\quad\mathop{\tilde{\curlyvee}}\nolimits^{\upkappa}[x\,{}^{q}_{p}]\geqslant\penalty|{\tilde{p}}-\penalty{\tilde{q}}|.

Hence

\displaystyle\measuredangle[\tilde{x}^{\prime}\,{}^{\tilde{p}}_{\tilde{q}}]

\displaystyle=\uppi-\text{\hbox to0.0pt{$\tilde{\text{\phantom{n}}}$\hss}{$\measuredangle$}}^{\upkappa}(x^{\prime}\,{}^{p}_{x}\hskip-0.86108pt)\geqslant\penalty

\displaystyle\geqslant\penalty\uppi-\measuredangle[x^{\prime}\,{}^{p}_{x}]=

\displaystyle=\measuredangle[x^{\prime}\,{}^{p}_{q}],

and
➌
follows.

Let us continue the proof.
Set
x_{0}=x
.
Let us apply inductively the above construction to get a sequence of hinges
[x_{n}\,{}^{p}_{q}]
with
x_{n+1}=x_{n}^{\prime}
.
From
➌
, we have that the sequence
s_{n}=\penalty\mathop{\tilde{\curlyvee}}\nolimits^{\upkappa}[x_{n}\,{}^{p}_{q}]
is nonincreasing.

The sequence might terminate at
x_{n}
only if
|{p}-\penalty{x_{n}}|+|{x_{n}}-\penalty{q}|<\penalty\tfrac{2}{3}{\hskip 0.5pt\cdot\penalty\hskip 0.5pt}\ell
.
In this case, by the assumptions of the lemma,
\mathop{\tilde{\curlyvee}}\nolimits^{\upkappa}[x_{n}\,{}^{p}_{q}]\geqslant\penalty|{p}-\penalty{q}|
.
Since the sequence
s_{n}
is nonincreasing, inequality
➊
follows.

Otherwise, the sequence
r_{n}=|{p}-\penalty{x_{n}}|+|{x_{n}}-\penalty{q}|
is nonincreasing and
r_{n}\geqslant\penalty\tfrac{2}{3}{\hskip 0.5pt\cdot\penalty\hskip 0.5pt}\ell
for all
n
.
Note that by construction, the distances

|{x_{n}}-\penalty{x_{n+1}}|
,
|{x_{n}}-\penalty{p}|
, and
|{x_{n}}-\penalty{q}|
are bounded away from zero for all large
n
.
Indeed, since on each step we move
x_{n}
toward to the point
p
or
q
that is further away, the distances
|{x_{n}}-\penalty{p}|
and
|{x_{n}}-\penalty{q}|
become about the same.
Namely, by
➍
, we have that
|{p}-\penalty{x_{n}}|-|{x_{n}}-\penalty{q}|\leqslant\penalty\tfrac{2}{9}{\hskip 0.5pt\cdot\penalty\hskip 0.5pt}\ell
for all large
n
.
Since
|{p}-\penalty{x_{n}}|+|{x_{n}}-\penalty{q}|\geqslant\penalty\tfrac{2}{3}{\hskip 0.5pt\cdot\penalty\hskip 0.5pt}\ell
, we have
|{x_{n}}-\penalty{p}|\geqslant\penalty\tfrac{\ell}{100}
and
|{x_{n}}-\penalty{q}|\geqslant\penalty\tfrac{\ell}{100}
.
Further, since
r_{n}\geqslant\penalty\tfrac{2}{3}{\hskip 0.5pt\cdot\penalty\hskip 0.5pt}\ell
,
➍
implies that
|{x_{n}}-\penalty{x_{n+1}}|>\tfrac{\ell}{100}
.

Since the sequence
r_{n}
is nonincreasing, it converges.
In particular,
r_{n}-r_{n+1}\to\penalty 0
as
n\to\penalty\infty
.
It follows that
\text{\hbox to0.0pt{$\tilde{\text{\phantom{n}}}$\hss}{$\measuredangle$}}^{\upkappa}(x_{n}\,{}^{p_{n}}_{x_{n+1}}\hskip-0.86108pt)\to\penalty\uppi
,
where
p_{n}=p
if
x_{n+1}\in\penalty[x_{n}q]
, and otherwise
p_{n}=q
.
Since
\measuredangle[x_{n}\,{}^{p_{n}}_{x_{n+1}}]\geqslant\penalty\text{\hbox to0.0pt{$\tilde{\text{\phantom{n}}}$\hss}{$\measuredangle$}}^{\upkappa}(x_{n}\,{}^{p_{n}}_{x_{n+1}}\hskip-0.86108pt)
, we have

\measuredangle[x_{n}\,{}^{p_{n}}_{x_{n+1}}]\to\penalty\uppi
as
n\to\penalty\infty
.

It follows that

r_{n}-s_{n}=|{p}-\penalty{x_{n}}|+|{x_{n}}-\penalty{q}|-\mathop{\tilde{\curlyvee}}\nolimits^{\upkappa}[x_{n}\,{}^{p}_{q}]\to\penalty 0.

(Here we used that
\ell\leqslant\penalty\varpi{}^{{\upkappa}}
.)
Together with the triangle inequality

|{p}-\penalty{x_{n}}|+|{x_{n}}-\penalty{q}|\geqslant\penalty|{p}-\penalty{q}|

this yields

\lim_{n\to\penalty\infty}\mathop{\tilde{\curlyvee}}\nolimits^{\upkappa}[x_{n}\,{}^{p}_{q}]\geqslant\penalty|{p}-\penalty{q}|.

Applying monotonicity of the sequence
s_{n}=\mathop{\tilde{\curlyvee}}\nolimits^{\upkappa}[x_{n}\,{}^{p}_{q}]
, we obtain
➊
.
∎

The final part of the proof above resembles the
cat’s cradle construction
introduced by the first author and Richard Bishop
[
6
]
.

The following lemma works in all complete spaces; it will be used as a substitute for the existence of a minimum point of a continuous function on a compact space.

8.36. Lemma on almost minimum.

Let
\mathcal{X}
be a complete metric space.
Suppose
r\colon\mathcal{X}\to\penalty\mathbb{R}
is a function,
p\in\penalty\mathcal{X}
, and
\upvarepsilon>0
.
Assume that the function
r
is strictly positive in
\overline{\mathrm{B}}{}[p,\tfrac{1}{\upvarepsilon^{2}}{\hskip 0.5pt\cdot\penalty\hskip 0.5pt}r(p)]
and

\varliminf_{n}r(x_{n})>0
for any convergent sequence

x_{n}\to\penalty x\in\penalty\overline{\mathrm{B}}{}[p,\tfrac{1}{\upvarepsilon^{2}}{\hskip 0.5pt\cdot\penalty\hskip 0.5pt}r(p)]
.

Then there is a point
p^{*}\in\penalty\overline{\mathrm{B}}{}[p,\tfrac{1}{\upvarepsilon^{2}}{\hskip 0.5pt\cdot\penalty\hskip 0.5pt}r(p)]
such that

(a)

r(p^{*})\leqslant\penalty r(p)
and

(b)

r(x)>(1-\upvarepsilon){\hskip 0.5pt\cdot\penalty\hskip 0.5pt}r(p^{*})

for any
x\in\penalty\overline{\mathrm{B}}{}[p^{*},\tfrac{1}{\upvarepsilon}{\hskip 0.5pt\cdot\penalty\hskip 0.5pt}r(p^{*})]
.

Proof.

Assume the statement is wrong.
Then for any
x\in\penalty\mathrm{B}{}(p,\tfrac{1}{\upvarepsilon^{2}}{\hskip 0.5pt\cdot\penalty\hskip 0.5pt}r(p))
with
r(x)\leqslant\penalty r(p)
, there is a point
x^{\prime}\in\penalty\mathcal{X}
such that

|{x}-\penalty{x^{\prime}}|<\tfrac{1}{\upvarepsilon}{\hskip 0.5pt\cdot\penalty\hskip 0.5pt}r(x)\quad\text{and}\quad r(x^{\prime})\leqslant\penalty(1-\upvarepsilon){\hskip 0.5pt\cdot\penalty\hskip 0.5pt}r(x).

Take
x_{0}=p
and consider a sequence of points
x_{n}
such that
x_{n+1}=\penalty x_{n}^{\prime}
.
Clearly

|{x_{n+1}}-\penalty{x_{n}}|\leqslant\penalty\tfrac{r(p)}{\upvarepsilon}{\hskip 0.5pt\cdot\penalty\hskip 0.5pt}(1-\upvarepsilon)^{n}\quad\hbox{and}\quad r(x_{n})\leqslant\penalty r(p){\hskip 0.5pt\cdot\penalty\hskip 0.5pt}(1-\upvarepsilon)^{n}.

In particular,
|{p}-\penalty{x_{n}}|<\tfrac{1}{\upvarepsilon^{2}}{\hskip 0.5pt\cdot\penalty\hskip 0.5pt}r(p)
.
Therefore the sequence
x_{n}
is Cauchy,

x_{n}\to\penalty x\in\penalty\overline{\mathrm{B}}{}[p,\tfrac{1}{\upvarepsilon^{2}}{\hskip 0.5pt\cdot\penalty\hskip 0.5pt}r(p)]

and

\lim_{n}r(x_{n})=0
, a contradiction.
∎

Proof of the globalization theorem (
8F
).

Exactly the same argument as in the proof of Theorem
8B

shows that
\mathcal{L}
is G-delta geodesic.
By Theorem
8F
,
for any hinge
[x\,{}^{p}_{y}]
in
\mathcal{L}
the angle
\measuredangle[x\,{}^{p}_{y}]
is defined
and

\measuredangle[x\,{}^{p}_{y}]+\measuredangle[x\,{}^{p}_{z}]\leqslant\penalty\uppi

for any two adjacent hinges.

Let us denote by
\mathop{\rm ComRad}\nolimits(p,\mathcal{L})

(which stands for
comparison radius
of
\mathcal{L}
at
p
)
the maximal value (possibly
\infty
) such that the comparison

\measuredangle[x\,{}^{p}_{y}]\geqslant\penalty\text{\hbox to0.0pt{$\tilde{\text{\phantom{n}}}$\hss}{$\measuredangle$}}^{\upkappa}(x\,{}^{p}_{y}\hskip-0.86108pt)

holds for any hinge
[x\,{}^{p}_{y}]
with
|{p}-\penalty{x}|+|{x}-\penalty{y}|<\mathop{\rm ComRad}\nolimits(p,\mathcal{L})
.

As follows from
8F
,
\mathop{\rm ComRad}\nolimits(p,\mathcal{L})>0
for any
p\in\penalty\mathcal{L}
and

\varliminf_{n\to\penalty\infty}\mathop{\rm ComRad}\nolimits(p_{n},\mathcal{L})>0

for any converging sequence of points
p_{n}\to\penalty p
.
That makes it possible to apply the lemma on almost minimum (
8F
) to the function
p\mapsto\penalty\mathop{\rm ComRad}\nolimits(p,\mathcal{L})
.

According to the short hinge lemma (
8E
), it is sufficient to show that

s_{0}=\inf_{p\in\penalty\mathcal{L}}\mathop{\rm ComRad}\nolimits(p,\mathcal{L})\geqslant\penalty\varpi{}^{{\upkappa}}\quad\text{for any}\quad p\in\penalty\mathcal{L}.

➏

We argue by contradiction, assuming that
➏
does not hold.

The rest of the proof is easier for geodesic spaces
and easier still for compact spaces.
Thus we give three different arguments for each of these cases.

Compact case.

Assume
\mathcal{L}
is compact.

By Theorem
8F
,
s_{0}>0
.
Take a point
p^{*}\in\penalty\mathcal{L}
such that
r^{*}=\penalty\mathop{\rm ComRad}\nolimits(p^{*},\mathcal{L})
is sufficiently close to
s_{0}

(
p^{*}
such that
s_{0}\leqslant\penalty\penalty r^{*}<\penalty\min\{\varpi{}^{{\upkappa}},\tfrac{3}{2}{\hskip 0.5pt\cdot\penalty\hskip 0.5pt}s_{0}\}
will do).
Then the key lemma (
8F
) applied to
p^{*}
and
\ell
slightly bigger than
r^{*}
(say, such that
r^{*}<\ell<\min\{\varpi{}^{{\upkappa}},\tfrac{3}{2}{\hskip 0.5pt\cdot\penalty\hskip 0.5pt}s_{0}\}
) implies that

\measuredangle[x\,{}^{p^{*}}_{q}]\geqslant\penalty\text{\hbox to0.0pt{$\tilde{\text{\phantom{n}}}$\hss}{$\measuredangle$}}^{\upkappa}(x\,{}^{p^{*}}_{q}\hskip-0.86108pt)

for any hinge
[x\,{}^{p^{*}}_{q}]
such that
|{p^{*}}-\penalty{x}|+|{x}-\penalty{q}|<\ell
.
Thus
r^{*}\geqslant\penalty\ell
, a contradiction.

Geodesic case.

Assume
\mathcal{L}
is geodesic.

Fix a small
\upvarepsilon>0
(
\upvarepsilon=0.0001
will do).
Apply the lemma on almost minimum (
8F
) to find a point
p^{*}\in\penalty\mathcal{L}
such that

r^{*}=\penalty\mathop{\rm ComRad}\nolimits(p^{*},\mathcal{L})<\penalty\varpi{}^{{\upkappa}}

and

\mathop{\rm ComRad}\nolimits(q,\mathcal{L})>\penalty(1-\upvarepsilon){\hskip 0.5pt\cdot\penalty\hskip 0.5pt}r^{*}

➐

for any
q\in\penalty\overline{\mathrm{B}}{}[p^{*},\tfrac{1}{\upvarepsilon}{\hskip 0.5pt\cdot\penalty\hskip 0.5pt}r^{*}]
.

Applying the key lemma (
8F
) for
p^{*}
and
\ell
slightly bigger than
r^{*}
leads to a contradiction.

General case.

Let us construct
p^{*}\in\penalty\mathcal{L}
as in the previous case.
Since
\mathcal{L}
is not geodesic, we cannot apply the key lemma directly.
Instead, let us pass to the ultrapower
\mathcal{L}^{\text{\rm}}
, which
is a geodesic space (see
4C
).

In Theorem
8F
,
inequality
➐
implies that
condition
8F
holds for some fixed
R_{\hbox{\rm\scriptsize\ref{SHORT.curv>=k}}}=\tfrac{r^{*}}{100}>0
at any point
q\in\penalty\overline{\mathrm{B}}{}[p^{*},\tfrac{1}{2{\hskip 0.5pt\cdot\penalty\hskip 0.5pt}\upvarepsilon}{\hskip 0.5pt\cdot\penalty\hskip 0.5pt}r^{*}]\subset\penalty\mathcal{L}
.
Therefore a similar statement is true in the ultrapower
\mathcal{L}^{\text{\rm}}
;
that is,
for any point

q_{\text{\rm}}\in\penalty\overline{\mathrm{B}}{}[p^{*},\tfrac{1}{2{\hskip 0.5pt\cdot\penalty\hskip 0.5pt}\upvarepsilon}{\hskip 0.5pt\cdot\penalty\hskip 0.5pt}r^{*}]\subset\penalty\mathcal{L}^{\text{\rm}}
,
condition
8F
holds for, say,
R_{\hbox{\rm\scriptsize\ref{SHORT.curv>=k}}}=\tfrac{r^{*}}{101}
.

Note that
r^{*}\geqslant\penalty\mathop{\rm ComRad}\nolimits(p^{*},\mathcal{L}^{\text{\rm}})
.
Therefore we can apply the lemma on almost minimum
at the point
p^{*}
to the function
x\mapsto\penalty\penalty\mathop{\rm ComRad}\nolimits(x,\mathcal{L}^{\text{\rm}})

and
\upvarepsilon^{\prime}=\sqrt{\upvarepsilon}=0.01
.

For the resulting point
p^{{*}{*}}\in\penalty\mathcal{L}^{\text{\rm}}
, we have
r^{{*}{*}}=\penalty\mathop{\rm ComRad}\nolimits(p^{{*}{*}},\mathcal{L})<\penalty\varpi{}^{{\upkappa}}
,
and

\mathop{\rm ComRad}\nolimits(q_{\text{\rm}},\mathcal{L}^{\text{\rm}})>\penalty(1-\upvarepsilon^{\prime}){\hskip 0.5pt\cdot\penalty\hskip 0.5pt}r^{{*}{*}}
for any
q_{\text{\rm}}\in\penalty\overline{\mathrm{B}}{}[p^{{*}{*}},\tfrac{1}{\upvarepsilon^{\prime}}{\hskip 0.5pt\cdot\penalty\hskip 0.5pt}r^{{*}{*}}]
.
Thus applying the key lemma (
8F
) for
p^{{*}{*}}
and for
\ell
slightly bigger than
r^{{*}{*}}
leads to a contradiction.
∎

G
Properties of geodesics and angles

Remark.

All proofs in this section can be easily modified to use only the local definition of
CBB
spaces without use of the globalization theorem (
8F
).

8.37. Geodesics do not split.

In a
CBB
space, geodesics do not bifurcate.

More precisely, let
\mathcal{L}
be a
CBB
space and
[px]
,
[py]
be two geodesics. Then:

(a)

If there is
\upvarepsilon>0
such that
\mathrm{geod}_{[px]}(t)=\mathrm{geod}_{[py]}(t)

for all
t\in\penalty[0,\upvarepsilon)
,
then
[px]\subset\penalty[py]
or
[py]\subset\penalty[px]
.

(b)

If
\measuredangle[p\,{}^{x}_{y}]=0
, then
[px]\subset\penalty[py]
or
[py]\subset\penalty[px]
.

8.38. Corollary.

Let
\mathcal{L}
be a
CBB
space.
Then the restriction of any geodesic in
\mathcal{L}
to a proper segment is the unique minimal geodesic joining its endpoints.

In case
\upkappa\leqslant\penalty 0
, the proof is easier, since the model triangles are always defined.
To deal with
\upkappa>0
we have to argue locally.

Proof of
8G
;
8G
.

Let
t_{\max}
be the maximal value
such that
\mathrm{geod}_{[px]}(t)=\mathrm{geod}_{[py]}(t)
for all
t\in\penalty[0,t_{\max})
.
Since geodesics are continuous,
\mathrm{geod}_{[px]}(t_{\max})=\penalty\mathrm{geod}_{[py]}(t_{\max})
.
Let

q=\mathrm{geod}_{[px]}(t_{\max})=\penalty\mathrm{geod}_{[py]}(t_{\max}).

We must show that
t_{\max}=\min\{|{p}-\penalty{x}|,|{p}-\penalty{y}|\}
.

If that is not true, choose a sufficiently small
\upvarepsilon>0
such that the points

x_{\upvarepsilon}=\mathrm{geod}_{[px]}(t_{\max}+\upvarepsilon)\quad\text{and}\quad y_{\upvarepsilon}=\mathrm{geod}_{[py]}(t_{\max}+\upvarepsilon)

are distinct.
Let

z_{\upvarepsilon}=\mathrm{geod}_{[px]}(t_{\max}-\upvarepsilon)=\mathrm{geod}_{[py]}(t_{\max}-\upvarepsilon).

Clearly,
\text{\hbox to0.0pt{$\tilde{\text{\phantom{n}}}$\hss}{$\measuredangle$}}^{\upkappa}(q\,{}^{z_{\upvarepsilon}}_{x_{\upvarepsilon}}\hskip-0.86108pt)=\text{\hbox to0.0pt{$\tilde{\text{\phantom{n}}}$\hss}{$\measuredangle$}}^{\upkappa}(q\,{}^{z_{\upvarepsilon}}_{y_{\upvarepsilon}}\hskip-0.86108pt)=\uppi
.
Thus from the
\text{\rm CBB}({\upkappa})
comparison (
8A
),
\text{\hbox to0.0pt{$\tilde{\text{\phantom{n}}}$\hss}{$\measuredangle$}}^{\upkappa}(q\,{}^{x_{\upvarepsilon}}_{y_{\upvarepsilon}}\hskip-0.86108pt)=0
and thus
x_{\upvarepsilon}=y_{\upvarepsilon}
, a contradiction.

8G
.

From hinge comparison
8C
,

\measuredangle[p\,{}^{x}_{y}]=0\quad\Rightarrow\quad\text{\hbox to0.0pt{$\tilde{\text{\phantom{n}}}$\hss}{$\measuredangle$}}^{\upkappa}\left(p\,{}^{\mathrm{geod}_{[px]}(t)}_{\mathrm{geod}_{[py]}(t)}\hskip-0.86108pt\right)=0

and thus
{\mathrm{geod}_{[px]}(t)}={\mathrm{geod}_{[py]}(t)}
for all small
t
.
Therefore we can apply
8G
.
∎

8.39. Adjacent angle lemma.

Let
\mathcal{L}
be a
CBB
space.
Assume that two hinges
[z\,{}^{x}_{p}]
and
[z\,{}^{y}_{p}]
in
\mathcal{L}
are adjacent.
Then

\measuredangle[z\,{}^{p}_{y}]+\measuredangle[z\,{}^{p}_{x}]=\uppi.

Proof.

From the hinge comparison (
8C
) we have that both angles

\measuredangle[z\,{}^{p}_{y}]
and
\measuredangle[z\,{}^{p}_{x}]
are defined and

\measuredangle[z\,{}^{p}_{y}]+\measuredangle[z\,{}^{p}_{x}]\leqslant\penalty\uppi.

Clearly
\measuredangle[z\,{}^{x}_{y}]=\uppi
.
Thus the result follows from the triangle inequality for angles (
6C
).
∎

8.40. Angle semicontinuity.

Suppose
\mathcal{L}_{n}
is a sequence of
\text{\rm CBB}({\upkappa})
spaces and
\mathcal{L}_{n}\to\penalty\mathcal{L}_{\text{\rm}}
as
n\to\penalty\text{\rm}
.
Assume that a sequence of hinges
[p_{n}\,{}^{x_{n}}_{y_{n}}]
in
\mathcal{L}_{n}
converges to a hinge
[p_{\text{\rm}}\,{}^{x_{\text{\rm}}}_{y_{\text{\rm}}}]
in
\mathcal{L}_{\text{\rm}}
.
Then

\measuredangle[p_{\text{\rm}}\,{}^{x_{\text{\rm}}}_{y_{\text{\rm}}}]\leqslant\penalty\lim_{n\to\penalty\text{\rm}}\measuredangle[p_{n}\,{}^{x_{n}}_{y_{n}}].

Proof.

From
8C
,

\measuredangle[p_{\text{\rm}}\,{}^{x_{\text{\rm}}}_{y_{\text{\rm}}}]=\sup\left\{\,{\text{\hbox to0.0pt{$\tilde{\text{\phantom{n}}}$\hss}{$\measuredangle$}}^{\upkappa}(p_{\text{\rm}}\,{}^{\bar{x}_{\text{\rm}}}_{\bar{y}_{\text{\rm}}}\hskip-0.86108pt)}\ {:}\ {\bar{x}_{\text{\rm}}\in\penalty\mathopen{]}p_{\text{\rm}}x_{\text{\rm}}],\ \bar{y}_{\text{\rm}}\in\penalty\mathopen{]}p_{\text{\rm}}x_{\text{\rm}}]}\,\right\}.

For fixed
\bar{x}_{\text{\rm}}\in\penalty\mathopen{]}p_{\text{\rm}}x_{\text{\rm}}]

and
\bar{y}_{\text{\rm}}\in\penalty\mathopen{]}p_{\text{\rm}}x_{\text{\rm}}]
,
choose
\bar{x}_{n}\in\penalty\mathopen{]}px_{n}]
and
\bar{y}_{n}\in\penalty\mathopen{]}py_{n}]
so that
\bar{x}_{n}\to\penalty\bar{x}_{\text{\rm}}

and
\bar{y}_{n}\to\penalty\bar{y}_{\text{\rm}}
as
n\to\penalty\text{\rm}
.
Clearly

\text{\hbox to0.0pt{$\tilde{\text{\phantom{n}}}$\hss}{$\measuredangle$}}^{\upkappa}(p_{n}\,{}^{\bar{x}_{n}}_{\bar{y}_{n}}\hskip-0.86108pt)\to\penalty\text{\hbox to0.0pt{$\tilde{\text{\phantom{n}}}$\hss}{$\measuredangle$}}^{\upkappa}(p_{\text{\rm}}\,{}^{\bar{x}_{\text{\rm}}}_{\bar{y}_{\text{\rm}}}\hskip-0.86108pt)

as
n\to\penalty\text{\rm}
.

From the hinge comparison (
8C
),
